# Review log: Session suspend, idle suspension, and resume driver

## Standing context

**Changelog (compaction pass, 2026-10-09).** This pass read the whole ledger: the original run (spec rounds 1 to 6, redesign 1 and 2), the redesign-2 run (spec rounds 1 and 2), the redesign-3 run (spec rounds 1 to 11), the f1 human-decision and reconcile entries, and non-spec rounds 1 to 16. It rewrote all four sections.
- Lifted: every live DECISION, FACT, WATCHOUT, MISTAKE, OPEN, UNVERIFIED, and unclosed DEFERRED. Facts repeated across rounds (anchor resolution, identifier sweeps, the plan-deliverable map, values.schema.json, `podHeld` state reading) are merged into one line each.
- Closed and dropped from Open: OD-1, OD-A, and OD-R2 (resolved in f1.human-decisions); the inbox-only `suspended` and durable-`EXPIRE` OPENs (Settled decisions); per-child budget keys (SPEC-5o covers every key); SPEC-5m restatement; `clearInboxOnResume` `coordinator_failover` (function deleted); CH-CHECKPOINT/CH-BARRIER (SPEC-2c item 7); §15.4 Ordering guarantee (SPEC-5i2 item 4); `completeResume` ambiguous commit (CODE-4 items 5.2, 5.3); request-context held delivery (CODE-4 item 6); tier-11 register test (TEST-0); release-guard ABA test (non-spec r1); CODE-5 non-empty `PodAssignment` (state-aware `podHeld`); the §10.1.1 terminal-bump OPEN (decided: the bump stays in §7.2 step 4); every checklist DEFERRED (reconcile.index-checklist.1) and the non-spec DEFERREDs on budget TTL, PostAgentOutput, and the MCP interrupt write.
- Corrected through CORRECTS: held-message delivery no longer claims at-least-once; "every entry to `suspended` restamps `SuspendedAt`" holds only after CODE-6 item 1 routes the MCP interrupt; budget-key expiry "fails closed" holds for the spec only; the §4.7.1 `CoordinatorFence` row is a staged site; SPEC-3b names `status_change`; the interrupt-suspension "waits for the client" wording describes the backlog predicate only.
- Deleted superseded traps: "do not file CH-CHECKPOINT/CH-BARRIER", "§4.7.1 row stays true", "do not file §10.1.2 step 3", the at-most-once/idle-refusal restore trap (its replacement was itself replaced by redesign 3), and the forward-era inbox-buffering OPENs.
- Disagreement kept newer: non-spec.3 fix-G2 (the refusal reads the Redis `leasestore.Lease.Holder`) over fix-design-G2 (`Lease.CoordinatorReplica`).
- Target: not reached. The section is 252 lines (109 Settled, 86 Traps, 34 Open, and 1 Deferred entry) because Traps carries every live WATCHOUT and MISTAKE with its reason; dropping them would lose traps later rounds cite.

### Settled

**Incarnation identity.** A `resuming` incarnation is the `coordination_generation` value its `resume_pending → resuming` write increments to; every non-terminal exit is conditioned on `resuming` at that value (SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**). Rejected: state plus unchanged generation (ABA), `UpdatedAt` token (`AdvanceLastSeq` rewrites it after each Publish), `recovery_generation` (client-visible), a new column.
**Deleted SPEC-1b claims.** "At most one attempt per session at a time" and "an attempt ends by the `resuming` watchdog deadline" are deleted; both were false and no rule depends on them.
**`sweepResuming` exit.** Guarded on state plus the listed row's generation, retry branch computed from the locked row, `errResumingSuperseded` on a miss (CODE-4 item 15).
**Entry CAS.** The entry write increments under CAS on the value read before the lease was confirmed, so two drivers cannot both enter and a lease taken without `RecordHandoff` is harmless.
**Single gateway releaser.** The replacement pod is in neither `PodAssignment` nor the registry before the commit (CODE-4 item 4); the attempt is the only gateway releaser.
**Orphan-claim GC.** The WPC orphan-claim GC reclaims a stopped attempt's pod; its drain of a live attempt older than `claimOrphanTimeout` is the accepted outcome in SPEC-1b; CODE-4 item 19 puts the timeout guidance in `--max-resuming-seconds` help; the spec states no ordering of the two timeouts.
**No post-exit bump.** `bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose` in `applyFailureFromResuming` is deleted; F-7.1.14 stays closed through the terminal-write bump.
**Generation incrementers.** Three writes advance `coordination_generation`: the takeover CAS (§10.1.2 step 1), the resume entry (SPEC-1b), and the mid-resume terminal collapse (§7.2 snapshot-close step 4(a)). §10.1.1 **Generation counters:** (SPEC-1f item 2) holds the baseline and the first two; the terminal bump stays in §7.2 step 4.
**§4.2 pointer.** SPEC-1f item 1 makes §4.2 a field description plus the bare pointer "See §10.1.1 **Generation counters:**."; SPEC-1f also rewrites the "first coordinator handoff mints 2" sentence.
**Never-advanced stamp.** SPEC-1f item 2 keeps 0076's baseline-stamp clause, reworded to "a session that no takeover and no resume driver write has advanced".
**§10.1.5 trigger.** SPEC-1f items 3 and 4 delete "or from a failed Postgres CAS on the session row" from §10.1.5 and §29.8 step 10; only a pod's generation-stale rejection triggers §10.1.5; only `coordfence` implements it in the tree.
**§10.1.5 no-pod paragraph.** SPEC-1f item 3 adds **Session with no bound pod.**; the sentence "A compare-and-swap that lands after the attempt's write ends that incarnation" is deleted.
**Sweeper generation.** The Sweeper compares the row with the binding's own generation stamp, and `RecordHandoff` is a CAS on the listed row's generation (CODE-5 items 2a, 2b); the `renewedGeneration` map is deleted.
**Adoption read.** SPEC-2a ties adoption to the §10.1.2 step 0 read; SPEC-2b widens step 0 to read `state` and `pod_assignment` in the same SELECT (lead sentence, SELECT literal, fallback "every column this step reads") and reduces §29.8 step 4 to a citation of step 0.
**`resuming` not adopted.** SPEC-2a says every replica evaluates the §6.2 `resuming` watchdog from the session row; the watchdog exits to `resume_pending` or, with retries exhausted, `awaiting_client_action`.
**Lease retention.** Under SPEC-2a, a session past `ready` holds REG-COORDLEASE only while it has a bound pod or pending driver work (`starting`, `running`, `input_required`, held-pod `suspended`, `resume_pending`, and `resuming`), and in `awaiting_client_action`, where 0091's retention stands (bounded at 900 s, and the holder's driver restores at once after a resume call). Podless `suspended`, like `created`, `finalizing`, and `ready`, holds no lease and has no coordinator. SPEC-2d serves its §29.3 terminate, delete, and resolution rows on the serving replica, and the events JSON row is served from `CH-EVENTRELAY` in every state. No on-demand adoption exists. The Sweeper releases a podless row's mirror row while it is still the prior holder (CODE-5 item 1).
**Fence-skip home.** §10.1.2 step 2 (SPEC-2b, retitled "steps 2 and 3") is the one home of the podless skip and also conditions step 3's "Because fence confirmation is required"; SPEC-2a reads only "The adopter runs the §10.1.2 handoff protocol."
**SPEC-2c sites.** Each fence-as-universal restatement becomes a §10.1.2 citation: §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH and CH-FENCE, §28.6, the §28.8 CH-ATTACH and CH-CHECKPOINT rows (item 5), §29.4 step 4, §29.5 step 3 (item 1), the §4.7.1 `CoordinatorFence` row (item 6, docs mirror in DOCS-1b), and the CH-CHECKPOINT and CH-BARRIER Preconditions (item 7).
**No fence at bind.** No fence-on-claim exists; the driver's row writes are generation-conditioned and code fences after the commit (CODE-4 item 5.4).
**Pre-attach collapse.** SPEC-1e keys the §7.2 pre-attach collapse on the `resume_pending → resuming` write.
**Retry accounting.** SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.** spends a retry on a pod failure, a failure the driver reports after its `resuming` write, or the watchdog and ends "No other entry into `resume_pending` advances it."; "`maxRetries` is the only retry budget" is deleted everywhere; CODE-2 is "Retry accounting"; `enterResumePending` leaves `RetryCount` unchanged.
**Route list home.** The routes into recovery are listed once, in the SPEC-1b **Resume driver.** opening paragraph.
**SPEC-3o.** A pointer at **Resume driver.** items 1 and 2.
**§29.6 podless resume.** An untraced entry point (SPEC-3k); SPEC-3m sends every `suspended` call out of the trace; Preconditions stay `awaiting_client_action`.
**Traversal home.** §8.10 owns the delegation-tree traversal; SPEC-3j cites it beside §7.3.
**SPEC-3b frame.** The REST resume row names `status_change`; `state_change` is the adapter-facing SessionEventKind.
**Held-pod resume.** No pod RPC: SPEC-3j and the Design SPEC-3 paragraph say the coordinator writes `suspended → running` and delivers the held messages per path 6.
**Off-holder held-pod `/resume`.** Fails closed with `TARGET_NOT_READY` (CODE-4 item 8), details `currentState` alone, because the spec names no details key for the coordinating replica; RECORDS-1 7(b) records the missing forward.
**OD-1 resolved.** The §6.2 `resuming` timeout bullet stays unchanged; the anchor drift is accepted in the non-spec edge case **Watchdog anchor.**, its one home.
**OD-A resolved.** The synchronous `StampSync` admission write before every `PodExecutor.Send` (CODE-6 items 5.1, 5.2).
**OD-R2 resolved.** The durable-inbox `EXPIRE` on `resume_pending` is deleted (SPEC-5z item 6, CODE-4 item 18, `RedisInbox.Expire` deleted).
**`ReattachNode`.** Stops calling `resumeOnPod` (CODE-4 item 11); only `completeResume` publishes a binding.
**Custody home.** SPEC-5i §7.2 delivery path 6: synchronous delivery only for a REST message the coordinating replica serves while the row names a pod; every other message is held in the DLQ before any state write; the coordinating replica's suspended sweep runs the backlog resume for a held-pod row, and each hold for a podless row is followed by the guarded `resume_pending` write.
**Held-message delivery.** SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.**: `deliverBuffered` peeks the older of the inbox and DLQ heads, removes an entry only after its `Send`, runs at every commit to `running` and before every path-2 delivery, and expires nothing. It states no at-least-once sentence (summary decision 18 says "level-triggered").
**Failed held delivery.** The replica stops and the entry stays at the head of its queue.
**Terminal race.** `holdMessage` re-reads after the enqueue and removes its own entries only on a terminal or failed re-read; SPEC-5i **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** opens with the failed-DLQ-enqueue answer (`error`/`inbox_unavailable`, no state write).
**Receipt home.** The path-6 DLQ-enqueue receipt is stated in **Held messages.** (`queued`/`dropped`) plus **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.**; **Synchronous delivery.** and **Delivery of held messages.** point at it.
**§15.4 `delivery` table.** SPEC-5k leaves the `"queued"` row unedited; its sentence below the table overrides the whole row for a `suspended` target.
**`inbox_unavailable`.** SPEC-5z item 9 widens the §15.4 row to "a durable-inbox or DLQ enqueue failed" plus a failed re-read, and drops "for durable inbox" from the status paragraph.
**DLQ TTL home.** The last sentence of **Delivery of held messages.**: TTL counted from acceptance, enforced only in `resume_pending`, `resuming`, and `awaiting_client_action`; the §15.4 `dlq_ttl_expired` row cites path 6 (SPEC-5z item 2); CODE-6 item 12 adds `resuming` to `sweepDLQExpiry` and does not sweep `suspended`.
**Reason definitions.** §15.4 is the only home; SPEC-5z item 8 reduces the §7.2 "For queued messages that later expire" glosses to bare names; item 5 deletes the §15.4 trimmer sentence (home: the §7.2 durable Per-message TTL row, item 4).
**Terminal drain.** SPEC-5z item 1 covers every DLQ entry whatever the state of its enqueue.
**Backlog resume.** Counts the inbox only for an `Idle` suspension and, for other reasons, only DLQ entries accepted after `SuspendedAt` (CODE-6 item 2, the **`HeldLen`.** paragraph, `CountAcceptedAfter`); no migration on entry to `suspended`; its last sentence ("held until the client acts") is deleted.
**§29.4 step 9.** SPEC-5y: "until a message or a resume call resumes it (§7.2 delivery path 6), or the session ends".
**Resume before release.** SPEC-5b's release sentence carries "unless it resumes the session for held messages (§7.2 delivery path 6 **Backlog resume.**)"; SPEC-5f drops "at once"; SPEC-5a and SPEC-5h say "hold of zero"; CODE-6 item 2 runs the backlog resume before the release in the same tick.
**Pod-named condition.** The `suspended → running` condition (row names a pod) is stated once, in the path 6 lead; code: the `resumeHeldPod` guard and `errResumeHeldPodReleased`.
**Pod-released branch.** On `errResumeHeldPodReleased` the caller takes the pod-released branch (CODE-6 items 6, 7); the owner kept it on every path, and SPEC-5i states it (Decision 22).
**`send_message` receipt.** A `lenny/send_message` to a `suspended` target gets `queued` on both branches; only a REST message the coordinator serves with a held pod gets `delivered`.
**`clearInboxOnResume`.** Deleted; `inbox_cleared` at lease takeover is RECORDS-1 7(h).
**No `suspended` forward.** No off-holder forward exists for a `suspended` target; §28.5.4 and LNK-INTERREPLICA still name `ForwardMessage` correctly for the `running`/`input_required` rows.
**SPEC-5i2 pointers.** Item 1 replaces SPEC-1b item 4's FIFO sentence; item 3 adds a DLQ-keyed pointer in §7.2 **Sibling coordination patterns:** item 1; item 4 adds one to the §15.4 **Ordering guarantee:** (DLQ half; the path-2 half predates 0092 and is unfiled).
**One payload encoding.** The MCP buffering block marshals `sessionserver.MessagePayload{ID, Role: "user", Content}`; `deliverBuffered` decodes only `MessagePayload`, through a helper extracted from `parseMessageBatch`.
**Held-delivery context.** Each entry's `Send`, `recordDeliveredResponse`, and removal run on the server-lifetime context, and the caller's ctx is read only between entries (CODE-4 item 6); a shutdown mid-turn duplicate is RECORDS-1 7(i).
**PreMessageDelivery.** The MCP `PhasePreMessageDelivery` block moves ahead of `Classify` and gates both buffer branches; `deliverBuffered` runs no interceptor and stamps `From` through `senderFrom`, moved into the executor package.
**Held REJECT.** A PostAgentOutput REJECT on a held message writes the §16.7 audit row, publishes and transcribes nothing, removes the entry, and continues; an audit-write failure stops the loop with the entry in place.
**PostAgentOutput signature.** `runPostAgentOutput` and `recordDeliveredResponse` drop the writer and return parts, the REJECT result, and the audit error; `recordRouteRejection` splits into audit and envelope halves; `route.go` is a CODE-4 target.
**Refused delivery.** SPEC-5f: a refused held message stays held (citing **Delivery of held messages.**); any other refused message is routed again by the session's then-current state.
**Idle atomicity.** `StampSync` in `PodExecutor.Send` refuses a `suspended` row, and the `running → suspended` write is guarded on `LastAgentActivityAt`; the row lock serializes both.
**Idle rule home.** SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** holds the rule and default; SPEC-5m and the SPEC-5e `running` row are pointers.
**Idle clock reset.** Stated once in the §6.2 intro sentence that lists writes to `last_agent_activity_at` ("and on each entry to `running` from a state where the clock is paused"); CODE-6 item 8 points at it; it excludes `input_required`.
**`"idle"` trigger.** SPEC-5x deletes it; carriers in CODE-6 item 13 (schema enum, proto comment and generated binding, `runtimeops.go`, `adapterclient` comments); DOCS-1b edits the adapter-contract row; TEST-1 tier 3 asserts the narrowed enum; the schema-breaking gate does not walk `$defs`.
**Docs mirrors.** DOCS-1b mirrors SPEC-5w through the state-machines.md `suspended` row; SPEC-5y has no docs mirror.
**SPEC-5d2.** Deletes the `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` glosses at §6.2 **Pod failure while `suspended` (pod still held):** and §10.1 **Orphan session reconciliation:**.
**Release guard.** SPEC-5b step 2 conditions the binding clear on "the same entry to `suspended` with that pod bound"; CODE-6 item 4.3 guards `State == suspended && SuspendedAt.Equal(row.SuspendedAt) && PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment`.
**Budget keys.** SPEC-5o re-arms every budget key of the root's tree on entry to and exit from `suspended`; TTL default 691200 s at §8.3, §11.3, and §17.8.1 (SPEC-5o, 5n, 5s).
**Budget-key carrier.** CODE-6 item 14: `(*treebudget.Reserver).RearmTree` behind `BudgetKeyRearmer`, flag `--delegation-budget-key-ttl-seconds`, chart `delegation.budgetKeyTTLSeconds`, a startup refusal when the TTL does not exceed `--max-suspended-session-seconds`, and exit sites that include `applyFailureFromActive` from `suspended`; RECORDS-1 7(d) records only the unbuilt §8.3 sentinel.
**Interrupt single writer.** The MCP `lenny/interrupt_session` handler is deleted and the tool proxies `postByID(..., "/interrupt")` (CODE-6 item 1); the Go SDK decodes `Session`, localcli prints `ID State`, and both fakes return the REST body.
**Wiring helpers.** `wireTurnAdmitter(w.exec, activityStamper)` and `budgetKeyRearmer(*treebudget.Reserver)` in cmd/lenny-gateway, pinned by `TestTurnAdmitterAndBudgetRearmerWiring`.
**Suspended expiry miss.** `sweepSuspended` returns `errSuspendedSuperseded` on a guard miss (no write, counter, or notify), as CODE-4 item 15 does.
**`HeldLen` error.** `ResumeBacklog` logs it and returns true, so the row gets no release that tick, and the error is not returned (CODE-6 item 2, the **`ResumeBacklog`.** paragraph). In the release's backlog check, the error is logged and the release continues (CODE-6 item 4 step 3a).
**`ReleaseSuspendedPod`.** Runs off `Tick` on a goroutine with its own per-replica in-flight set; the checkpoint carries the §4.4.3 60 s deadline feeding the existing 60 s retry; `lockFlight` becomes context-aware (CODE-6 item 4 step 2).
**Idle default fallback.** CODE-6 item 9 deletes the age-cap fallback in `sessionidle` and in `Server.runtimeMaxClientIdle`; the playground test becomes `IdleIgnoresSessionAge` asserting 300.
**Release after the claim.** A hook error and a handshake-stage failure release the claim with no compensation: `podclaim.DeleteClaim` on `Claim` and `Bind`, and `releaseResumeSlot(leaked=false)` on `Resume`, where an empty slot ID deletes the claim and a slot pool releases the reservation only. CODE-4 item 20.4 routes every failure from the credential assignment on. `Bind` releases only its attempt's leases through `attemptScoped` and `PrepareResult.MintedLeaseIDs` (CODE-3 items 3 and 4). No `ErrOnClaimed` sentinel exists.
**Lost-commit release.** `Binder.ReleaseUncommitted`: `Release(result, "failed")` on an exclusive pool, `ReleaseSlot` on a slot pool, without the session-wide `releaseCredentials`; it releases the attempt's own leases from `BindResult.MintedLeaseIDs` (filled by `Bind` through `PrepareResult`, by `materializeSlot`, and by `Binder.Resume`).
**Resume credentials.** Option A (owner, 2026-10-09). The suspension release returns every lease, and every restore assigns new leases after the hook and `handshake` and before `cl.Resume`, under the attempt's token (SPEC-1g, CODE-4 item 20). The suspension release and the failure funnel's release take `slotAccountLocks` around `releaseExecutor` (CODE-6 item 4 step 4, CODE-4 item 22). Rejected: a lease-preserving suspension release.
**Owner answers.** OD-2: the edge is staged (Decision 20). OD-3: no edit (Decision 21). OD-B: immediate `resume_pending` on every path (Decision 22). OD-R1: delivery at the pre-running commit (Decision 23).
**Single lease acquire.** CODE-4 item 3.3 is the only acquire; the rebuild branch publishes through `publishBinding`; `resumeOnPod` no longer acquires on the checkpoint branch.
**Release before rebind.** The driver calls `releaseThenResumeOnPod(ctx, row, onClaimed)` (item 3.4), which returns what `resumeOnPod` returns; item 8 deletes `handleResume`'s call.
**`completeResume` context.** Runs on the server-lifetime context; the attempt deadline bounds the attempt only up to the call.
**Commit repeat.** Item 5.2 repeats the commit `Update` on a non-guard store error until it succeeds, the guard fails, or the server context ends.
**Commit-miss classification.** Home: item 5.3. A miss counts as landed only after a store error, with the row in a state `podHeld` admits (`running`, `input_required`, `suspended`) and naming the attempt's sandbox: at `G` run step 4 and the success counter and audit row, and the rest of the tail and delivery only when `running`; above `G` publish and release nothing; every other miss calls `ReleaseUncommitted`. Binderless: `RecoveryGeneration` above the row `DriveResume` received stands in for the sandbox and `G`.
**`bumpRecoveryGeneration`.** Deleted by CODE-4 item 4; the commit is then the only `RecoveryGeneration` writer.
**`holdsPod`.** `Server.holdsPod(row)` (`PodAssignment != ""` or no binder) is defined in CODE-4 item 8 (S12) and cited by CODE-6 item 6; it replaces every "names a pod" caller test.
**Binderless driver.** With no binder, `DriveResume` runs `completeResume` in place of `attemptResume`.
**Over-cap skip.** The over-cap driver skip lives only in CODE-4 item 1; CODE-1 item 5 keeps the `resumeHeldPod` check.
**Test landing rule.** A test listed under a code deliverable lands in that step at any tier, and an existing-test update lands in the step whose code breaks it; TEST-1 holds the other new tier-2+ tests and the tier-0/11 checks.
**TEST-0.** A test-lane step at S6, directly after S5, re-anchors `assertInterReplicaLinkMatchesSessionLifecycle` on the §28.5.4 `ForwardMessage` sentence; old S6 to S15 became S7 to S16.
**Migration 0182 test.** Tier 2: a `prodMigrationSchema` entry plus a CHECK-rejects-negative assertion; the runtimestore tier-2 update lands in CODE-2.
**spec-map rule.** "Remove every deleted test's entries ... and add the replacing driver tests in their place."
**Test homes.** Hold outcomes are table-driven by pointer to CODE-6 item 7; `TestResumeSuspendedFollowsItsBranches` owns the server `ResumeSuspended` branches; the `ResumeBacklog` deny path is in `TestSweepSuspendedResumesBacklog` on a held-pod `Idle` row; no-retry-spend sits on the `handleResume` bullet; interrupt parity is tier 3 in rest_mcp_consistency; the context rule is `TestDeliverBufferedCallerCancelMidTurnDeliversOnce`; the release-guard ABA case is tier 1; the post-hook slot-retry case is under CODE-4.
**Expiry hook test.** Tier-1 `TestExpiryCounterIdleReasonMaxIdleTime_spec_16_1_1` is rewritten onto a suspended row and asserts one call each of `OnSessionTerminal` and `OnSessionExpired`.
**Checklist.** S1 to S5 point at SPEC-1 to SPEC-5 with no section lists; S9 (CODE-5) depends on S1 and S2.
**Chart.** CODE-6 item 11 edits values.yaml only (no values.schema.json, whose `gateway` and `delegation` are open objects), regenerates `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml` with `make generate`, and S13 runs tier 11.
**Age docs and help.** CODE-1 Targets add `cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go` for the `--max-session-age-seconds` help; DOCS-1b names configuration.md `sessionPolicy.maxSessionAgeSeconds` and `session.maxSessionAgeSeconds` rows.
**Client-guide targets.** DOCS-1b names, on docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md, the `maxSessionAge` and `maxIdleTime` TTL rows, the diagram's `running → suspended` and `suspended` edges, the State Descriptions `suspended` row, the `maxSuspendedPodHold` row, and a new suspended-lifetime row.
**runtime-configuration.md.** DOCS-1b deletes `limits.maxIdleTimeSeconds` (a retired knob) and corrects the `maxClientIdleSeconds` description and default.
**Proposal shape.** One proposal; the split was rejected and recorded in problem-statement.md and summary decision 19.
**Impact rows.** 0077 (Draft; premise "suspended may remain indefinitely" falsified); 0079 (Implemented; nothing to do); 0085 (Draft; must rebase and drop `RESUME_FAILED`, the `/resume` persisted-row entry, and the `holdOrFailOnResumeError` retags); 0091 (Implemented; this proposal retires its `ErrHeld` hold and `RESUME_FAILED` answer).
**0091 lease behavior.** 0091 changed no REG-COORDLEASE write: `Sweep` still releases the lease on a dead bound connection, and a bound-less row keeps its lease through the `priorHolder == s.replicaID` renew (coordination.go:375); `releaseExecutor` does not release the coordination lease.
**Budget keys in the tree.** `treebudget.DefaultTTL` is 24 h, set by the reserve script only when `TTL == -1`; the only constructor passes 0; a missing key reads as 0 with no sentinel; `Restore`, the grant key, and the high-watermark key re-arm.
**`resume_pending` keeps the pod name.** The failure exits stamp the failed pod into `PodAssignment` (failure.go:337-338); the spec releases the binding on that transition (spec/28 §28.5.1), so "no bound pod" is read from state.
**DLQ scores.** Every DLQ enqueue, `MigrateInboxToDLQ` included, scores `EnqueuedAt + DefaultDLQTTL`, and `EnqueuedAt` is stamped after the classification re-read, so score order is acceptance order and `CountAcceptedAfter` holds apart from clock skew.
**Send and context.** `PodExecutor.Send` returns the ctx error mid-turn after the runtime consumed the envelope and mints a fresh envelope ID per send; a turn on the server-lifetime context is bounded by stream liveness.
**Gateway-caused stream ends.** `PodExecutor.Release` evicts the stream with `closedByGateway`, so the zero-hold release produces no failure report.
**Checkpoint deadline.** The production checkpointer sets no `Deadline`, so the gateway side of a checkpoint is unbounded unless a caller adds one.
**Commit witness.** `pgstore.Update` returns the `tx.Commit` error, so an error can follow a landed COMMIT (pgtenant.go:98-100).
**Suspension metrics.** `lenny_session_pod_released_during_suspension_total` and `lenny_session_suspension_checkpoint_failed_total` are cataloged with no emitter; CODE-6 item 4 is the first emitter and its test asserts the §16.1 labels.
**Anchors and sweeps.** Every staged spec anchor resolves once with whitespace collapsed (spec/28 and spec/29 are hard-wrapped); "then applies the", "No other values are valid.", the §29.8 step 4 opening, and the CH-CHECKPOINT/CH-BARRIER sentence are scoped by their edits. Identifier sweeps for `RESUME_FAILED`, `maxSessionRetries`, the idle default, the 259200 TTL, the `"idle"` trigger, and the fence universals find every spec site staged. Do not re-audit without a new diff.
**Plan scope.** The claimed plan entry is the single §10.2 "Session suspend-and-resume proposal" item (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:2474-2525); every deliverable is staged or declined (expired-pod recycle goes to the recycling follow-up, RECORDS-1 item 9); RECORDS-1 items 3 to 6 dispose of F-7.2.27, F-7.3.29, F-7.3.30, F-10.1.25, and F-10.1.26.
**Kubernetes surface.** The proposal adds no CRD field, status write, finalizer, webhook, or request-path reconcile; REG-COORDLEASE is a Redis key.

### Traps

**Do not restore the old SPEC-1b condition.** State plus unchanged generation is ABA-prone: the watchdog exit bumps nothing, so a re-entry restores the pair and a stale commit passes. The entry increment plus the incarnation condition replaces it.
**Do not restore `UpdatedAt` as guard or token.** The guard lost so every `resuming` exit uses one generation condition; the token lost because `AdvanceLastSeq` advances `UpdatedAt` before the commit.
**Do not restore the post-exit bump or the deleted SPEC-1b claims.** The next entry increments; two replicas claim before the entry write; the watchdog anchor moves with every row write.
**Terminal writers read `fromState` outside their mutator.** They bump the generation in a second Update (session_transitions.go); a §7.2 step 4 fidelity defect owned elsewhere; exclusivity holds because the commit requires `resuming`.
**Binding stamp.** `resume_rebind.go` stamped the pre-entry generation (`G−1`), which `reportSupersededByPeer` drops; CODE-4 item 5.4 restamps with `G`.
**No fence at bind, no fence-on-claim.** No stale coordinator addresses the replacement pod; §10.1.2 step 3 accepts RPCs on a pod with no fenced generation.
**Do not add a `resuming` guard to §10.1.2 step 1 or per acquirer.** SPEC-2a's step-0 tie plus SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.** carry exclusivity; do not edit step 1's 0-row handling.
**§10.1.2 step 2's bold "hard precondition" sentence needs no edit.** It follows SPEC-2b's "Otherwise," and is scoped to the bound-pod case.
**Fence-restatement sweeps.** Grep "acquired coordination", "CoordinatorFence` acknowledgement", and "fence acknowledgement" with lines joined across all of spec/; "must have received" (§29.5 step 3) hides from "must receive". §29.8 steps 6 to 9 trace a pod-bound session and need no edit.
**"No bound pod" is read from state.** `resume_pending` and `awaiting_client_action` rows name the failed pod in `PodAssignment`; CODE-5 `podHeld` decides by state. Do not refile SPEC-2b as "fences a dead pod"; test fixtures for recovering rows must carry the failed pod's name.
**Do not describe a pointer's target in SPEC-1f.** The round-1 pointer "its baseline and the writes that advance it" went false against the §7.2 terminal bump; keep the bare pointer and do not add the terminal bump to §10.1.1.
**Replaced: the replica-local inbox fallback for a `suspended` target.** It covered SPEC-5i, the §29.3 lead, `bufferTargetInbox`, and the MCP executor path. It lost because the default `MemoryInbox` is per replica and the coordinator never reads it; the DLQ is a shared Redis key. Do not reintroduce "inbox buffering" for a `suspended` target; the `running`/`input_required` fallback is RECORDS-1 7(f), deliberately unstaged.
**Replaced: the off-holder forward of a held-pod `suspended` message.** It lost because no inter-replica carrier exists and a forward with an ambiguous outcome followed by an enqueue delivers twice.
**Replaced: `DrainForResume` and `clearInboxOnResume`.** The drain fed messages back through the classifier and `DLQ.DrainAll` lost the rest on a failed send; `clearInboxOnResume` cleared nothing and emitted `inbox_cleared` with `coordinator_failover` on every restore.
**Replaced: the drain after a commit to `running`.** It went with `holdMessage`'s retract-and-re-route, `PopOldest`, `RemoveAll`, `Restore`, expiry inside the drain, the DLQ sweep of `suspended` rows, the state-free `dlq_ttl_expired` text, and `queued` on a failed re-read. It lost because a stopped drain stranded entries in a `running` DLQ, the retract raced the drain, and the `suspended` sweep raced the backlog resume.
**Do not restore** retract-after-drain, a restore of attempted DLQ entries, expiry in the delivery loop, the DLQ sweep of `suspended` rows, the durable inbox `EXPIRE`, an inbox-to-DLQ migration on entry to `suspended`, or a backlog resume keyed on an interrupt suspension's inbox or pre-suspension DLQ entries.
**Do not restore an at-least-once or duplicate-suppression sentence in SPEC-5i.** It claimed crash redelivery for the in-memory inbox, which §7.2 says is lost on crash; the §7.2 Durability and Explicit ACK rows own redelivery. Do not cite `delivered_message_ids` in staged text; no adapter code implements it.
**Never route a refused held entry by path 6 or re-enqueue it.** That duplicates inbox entries and breaks DLQ acceptance order; the entry stays at its head.
**Do not take the delivery lock in `SuspendIdle`, route admission through the asynchronous `Stamp`, or call `ResumeSuspended` holding the delivery lock.** The lock is per replica and misses `Send` callers outside the message paths; `Stamp` is coalesced.
**Replaced: idle atomicity from `TurnInFlight` and the guard alone, and `TryLock` in `SuspendIdle`.** No delivery stamps `LastAgentActivityAt`, the turn token is free between turns of one batch, and the lock misses other replicas and callers.
**Replaced: 0091's `renewedGeneration` record and the unconditional `RecordHandoff`.** The record goes stale across a same-replica rebind; the unconditional increment ends a live `resuming` incarnation.
**Replaced: a restore that assigns no credential lease.** `Binder.Resume` minted none while every release revoked the session's leases, so a restored runtime started with an empty credential file; §6.1 and §4.7.8 require a fresh assignment before `Resume`. Keep `AssignCredentials` after `handshake` and before `cl.Resume`.
**Replaced: `podsession.ErrOnClaimed`.** The retry loops already stop on an unwrapped hook error; do not re-add a sentinel.
**Replaced: the session-wide release on `Bind` reconnect and launch failures.** It revokes another attempt's leases; the `attemptScoped` request releases its own `MintedLeaseIDs`.
**Replaced: the unlocked suspension and funnel releases.** Both hold `slotAccountLocks` across `releaseExecutor` on both pool kinds; without it a same-replica resume loses its new leases.
**Do not re-add the pod-named condition to individual SPEC-5i bullets.** The path 6 lead governs every bullet, including the podless-miss outcome that Decision 22 adds to it.
**Do not restate a `message_expired` reason predicate in §7.2 or the §15.4 reason rows.** Each cites its home.
**Pre-running DLQ TTL is unenforced.** The commit to `running` delivers a pre-running session's DLQ (Decision 23); do not re-add pre-running to the TTL enforcement sentence.
**Do not restate "oldest first by acceptance time" in §7.2 item 1, and do not add a DLQ clause to the §15.4 `"queued"` cell.** Both recreate copies of path 6.
**An interrupt-suspended session does not wait for the client.** Any message from any sender resumes it under path 6; only the backlog sweep is limited to DLQ entries accepted after `SuspendedAt`. Do not add a sender condition to make "client acts" true.
**Enqueue before any state write is normative.** A later enqueue strands the message in a `running` DLQ; do not reword the bullets back to "writes ... and enqueues".
**Compare `SuspendedAt` with `time.Time.Equal`.** The row round-trips through timestamptz; do not restore the state-plus-pod release guard.
**Do not re-add a route list to Retry accounting, CODE-2, or the docs.** The one home is the **Resume driver.** opening.
**Do not renumber Resume driver items 1 to 4.** SPEC-3o cites items 1 and 2.
**Do not extend the idle-clock reset to `input_required`.**
**Do not sync SPEC-5f with SPEC-5i.** SPEC-5f is a routing pointer at path 6.
**"resume RPC".** The only `Resume` RPC restores from a checkpoint on a replacement pod; reintroducing the phrase for the held-pod path misleads an implementor.
**MISTAKE: renaming a path-6 action in one place.** Redesign 2 removed "the resume RPC" from SPEC-5i but left it in SPEC-3j and the Design SPEC-3 paragraph. When renaming, grep the whole proposal for the old phrase.
**MISTAKE: recording a regression the proposal causes as a pre-existing defect.** Earlier rounds filed the missing budget-key TTL carrier in RECORDS-1 7(d); CODE-1 and CODE-6 made the 24 h key expiry reachable, so the record could not close it and round 3 raised a security finding. Fix such a gap with a code carrier.
**MISTAKE: crediting 0091 with a lease keep-and-renew change.** CODE-4 item 14 claimed it; the shipped `priorHolder == s.replicaID` renew is what keeps the lease.
**MISTAKE: lettered RECORDS-1 cross-references lag after re-lettering.** The Lease mirror lag edge case cited 7(h) for the barrier finding; today 7(f) is the inbox fallback, 7(g) the barrier generation, 7(h) `inbox_cleared` at takeover, 7(i) the crash duplicate, 7(j) the durable trimmer. Keep cross-references as pointers.
**Glob trap.** `*spec-changes.md` also matches `*non-spec-changes.md`; use the full file name.
**Budget-key "fails closed" is a spec property only.** The reserve script reads a missing key as 0; do not cite the sentinel as an implemented control.
**`RearmTree` rules.** Never call it inside a store `Update` mutator or fail the transition on its error; it is post-commit and best-effort. It keys on the session's own id with `PurgeRoot`'s SCAN, so no root predicate is needed. `DefaultTTL`'s "refreshed on every reserve" comment is false.
**New edges out of `suspended`.** Add each to CODE-6 item 14's exit-site list; the rearmer test covers only listed sites.
**Typed nil.** `budgetKeyRearmer` must return untyped nil for a nil `*treebudget.Reserver`; `w.exec` is a `*executor.PodExecutor` only on the pod path, so `wireTurnAdmitter` type-asserts.
**Interrupt routing.** Do not keep both interrupt registrations (`RegisterTool` overwrites by name). Fakes in `mcp_test.go` and `session_verbs_test.go` must return the REST body. Do not add zero-`SuspendedAt` guards to items 2.1, 2.3, or 4.3.
**Wrong releases.** `ReclaimClaimed`, `Release`, `ReleaseSlot`, and `rollbackBinding` revoke every lease of the session; `failResume` needs the Resume-local `bindAttempt` token (a foreign token is answered SUPERSEDED). Do not use them for the lost commit or the `Bind` hook error, and do not add an explicit `DeleteClaim` beside `releaseResumeSlot` or `failResume`. On a slot pool `DeleteClaim` deletes a claim siblings share.
**Rebuild-branch leases.** The lost attempt's own leases are released by ID; do not "fix" leftover leases with a session-wide release.
**Lease acquire.** Do not add an acquire inside `releaseEarlierBinding` or `releaseThenResumeOnPod`; item 3.4 orders `releaseEarlierBinding`'s doc comment rewritten. A publish after a running commit uses `publishBinding`.
**S8 signature.** S8 (CODE-3 item 5) adds `onClaimed` to `resumeOnPod`; `releaseThenResumeOnPod` and `ReattachNode` pass nil until S12. `TestSlotAccountLockContextCancel_spec_5_2` uses the old `releaseThenResumeOnPod` signature.
**`recordRouteRejection`.** It writes the envelope unconditionally (a nil writer panics) and returns false on audit failure; `postagentoutput_internal_test.go` changes with the signature.
**Do not add PreMessageDelivery to the REST path.** §4.8 scopes it to `lenny/send_message`.
**MCP block comment F-MS5.** Rewrite the marshal and the comment; `MessagePayload.Content` keeps the wire form.
**Deadline safety.** A commit after the attempt deadline is safe only because step 2 is guarded on `resuming` and `G` and item 3.4 says correctness does not depend on the attempt ending first; keep both.
**Held-delivery context.** Do not add per-caller detached contexts; test the rule once at `deliverBuffered`.
**Never treat a non-guard `Update` error in `completeResume` as a guard miss.** The COMMIT may have landed and `ReleaseUncommitted` would free a pod the committed row names; the claim-orphan GC never reclaims a pod a committed row names.
**Landed-test traps.** On a concurrent pool `PodAssignment` is the shared pod and a recovering row keeps the failed pod's name, so keep both conditions (after a store error; a `podHeld` state). `podHeld` is unexported in coordination; name its state set and do not import it. Do not reintroduce a "recovery state" exclusion list (§6.2 and §15 define the term differently). `handleInterrupt`'s row-only fallback writes `suspended` unguarded and keeps `PodAssignment`.
**Commit repeat pacing.** Item 5.2's repeat states no backoff and runs with the delivery lock held; any fix reuses a jittered backoff.
**Coordinating-replica detail.** The `/resume` refusal carries `currentState` alone (CODE-4 item 8). Do not add a details key for the coordinating replica, which the spec does not name.
**`holdsPod` placement.** Define it in CODE-4 item 8; S11/S12 precede S13 and CODE-6 would not compile first. With no binder, "row names a pod" differs from "pod held".
**Off-holder `deliverBuffered`.** Fails in `streamFor` ("not bound") and leaves entries in place; not a delivery defect.
**`TurnInFlight` gap.** Between held entries of one loop it is false; the next `StampSync` refuses a `suspended` row and the `Idle` backlog resume delivers it.
**Gate tests use a held-pod row.** `ResumeSuspended`'s held-pod gate masks a missing upstream gate; every mcptools test uses a fake `SessionDelivery`, so server branches are pinned only by session-server tests. Do not re-add the off-holder `ResumeSuspended` clause to `TestSweepSuspendedResumesBacklog`.
**`HeldLen` test row.** Use a held-pod `Idle` row (hold zero); "keeps its pod" discriminates treat-as-zero, and "Tick returns nil" discriminates propagation.
**Replaced: per-outcome enumerations in the hold tests.** Each round found one more missing result; the tests are table-driven by pointer. Do not re-enumerate.
**Nil-on-miss idiom.** Any watchdog write that notifies after a guarded Update needs a sentinel; `sweepMaxAge` and `sweepIdle` keep the pre-existing double notify, which 0092 does not fix. `expiryCapture` discards `fromState`.
**Watchdog release test.** "Releases an `Idle` row on the first tick" stays correct after the release goes asynchronous; do not rewrite it to wait.
**`expiry_counter_test.go` rewrite.** Seed `SuspendedAt` on the suspended row, or item 2.1 expires it as `max_idle_time`.
**Tier-11 probe.** `build_gaps_stream_failure_records_currency_test.go` regexes the `isRunningPod` body; CODE-5 changes the probe in the same step and probes for the absence of `StateStarting` in `adoptedState`.
**Lane rules.** Spec-lane steps run no test tiers; a step naming a spec and a non-spec deliverable stops with bad-lane; a test step before S1 empties the spec prefix. Do not refile the S5 to S6 tier-11 window.
**validate-maps and lint-migrations.** A spec-map entry must land with its test (or be listed in spec-map-pending.txt); every migration number must appear under tests/tier2_component/migrations/.
**Generated files.** values.schema.json is generated from pkg/chart/values and `manifests.yaml` carries DO NOT EDIT; never hand-edit either. Do not stage a per-key minimum for `gateway.*`.
**Summary file lists.** They mirror each deliverable's Targets; mirror every Targets edit.
**CODE-3 tests.** May name only CODE-3 symbols; `onClaimed`, `errResumeSuperseded`, and the per-attempt flag belong under CODE-4.
**Docs targets on session-lifecycle.md.** Targets name items one by one; grep the page for `suspend` before closing a docs finding. Line 80's maxSessionAge clause stays true; its "all session timers" claim is a DOCS-1b target.
**Banned docs phrases.** `session_failure_outcome_doc_reconciliation_test.go` bans "suspended --> resume_pending : Pod failure or pod released" and "(pod released), or pod failure" and requires the retries-exhausted `suspended` edge in state-machines.md.
**Envelope pointer count.** The tier-11 check pins seven `#messageenvelope--unified-message-format` links in spec/07; re-check if a fix touches those lines.
**`runtimeMaxClientIdle`** reads only the runtime's `SessionPolicy`; pre-existing, outside 0092.
**`DriveResume` lease predicate.** The Design lease gate is the one home; CODE-4 item 2 still writes the predicate out.
**`holdMessage` mapping copies.** CODE-4 item 7 and both CODE-6 item 7 bullets agree; if one changes, reduce the others to "answer as CODE-4 item 7 states".
**Claim register.** Every claim-register row needs a §28 `spec_anchor`; 0092 RECORDS-1 item 8 stated only subjects when last checked.
**Refuted: the 7-day suspended population.** The Sweeper's per-row `List` and `Get` and the watchdog's `suspended` listing scale with it. They are refuted as a design choice with no spec bound, and the Sweeper's `List`/`Get` cost predates 0092 (RECORDS-1 item 7(l)). Lease renewals, mirror upserts, and the `HeldLen` probe do not scale with it: podless `suspended` holds no lease, the Sweeper releases its mirror row (SPEC-2a, CODE-5 item 1), and only the coordinating replica of a held-pod row and the releasing replica call `HeldLen` (CODE-6 items 2 and 4). Refile a lease, mirror, or `HeldLen` cost for podless `suspended` only with a code path that incurs one.
**Refuted: store-outage degradation.** The path-2 DLQ peek under a Redis outage (EXECUTOR_FAILURE inside the first 60 s) loses no message; refile only with a loss case.
**Refuted: expiry against delivery.** `sweepDLQExpiry` on a stale `resuming` listing can expire an entry the commit's `deliverBuffered` sends; weighed four times below the bar.
**Refuted: clock skew in the backlog predicate.** `EnqueuedAt` and `SuspendedAt` come from different replicas; the missed message waits for the next message or expires; nothing is lost.
**Refuted: GC races.** The GC lookup-then-drain race and the binding-clear-before-release window predate 0092 and fail closed; on a concurrent pod a crash mid-release leaks the slot count, as on every release path.
**Not filed, do not refile.** §27.6 `idle_timeout` (playground cookie record); §6.2 release step 3's exit list; §29.6 step 1; the rebuild `resumeMode`; `session_dlq_archive` replay; §8.10 node `expired`; the 10:59, 15:361, and §8.3 "safety net" glosses; §15.4 "queued (buffered in inbox)" and `queueDepth`; CreateSession `maxIdleSeconds`; docs/api/mcp.md `interrupt_session` output; SDK docstrings and openapi descriptions; the 07:277 cross-tenant clause (inside SPEC-5u); the §10.1.4 `AdapterTerminating` exit; the terminal-drain double `target_terminated`; the §6.2 "client-originated" qualifying list.
**Refuted: docs-mirror findings.** The skeptic refuted every "docs mirror not staged" finding; a docs-lens finding needs a staged docs edit contradicting the post-change spec or a missing companion for a new metric or alert.
**Help text.** A per-pool `maxClientIdleSeconds` above the platform default loosens the bound, while the flag help says it tightens it; help text only.

## Ledger

### [mechanism.podless-session-coordination.1]

CORRECTS [Standing context Settled "Lease retention."]: it stated renewal in every non-terminal state except `created`, `finalizing`, and `ready`, and cited the §29.3 terminate row as the reason. A podless `suspended` session holds no lease (SPEC-2a), and SPEC-2d corrects the terminate row, which listed `suspended` as a lease-holding state.
CORRECTS [Standing context Trap "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population"]: spec.8.fix-design-G1.1 said "Mirror upserts for podless rows stop with the renewal". Stopping upserts leaves the existing row unreleased, because the Sweeper releases a mirror row only for a terminal session. CODE-5 item 1 now releases it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:313, :491; pkg/gateway/coordination/barrier/wiring.go:104.
CORRECTS [spec.8.fix-G1.1 and spec.8.fix-design-G1.1 DECISIONs on on-demand adoption]: on-demand adoption is deleted from SPEC-2a with RECORDS-1 item 7(l) and the summary Defects bullet. Every §29.3 route a podless `suspended` session admits has a form the serving replica serves alone. The rejected alternative "events JSON still needs a holder" was wrong, because the JSON form reads relay history on any replica. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/events.go:432 serveEventsJSON → pkg/gateway/session/sessionevents/events.go:386-424 (relay.History merge); cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:73.
CORRECTS [spec.8.fix-design-G1.1 WATCHOUT on the on-demand adopter's empty event replay buffer]: moot, because no on-demand adopter exists.
CORRECTS [spec.8.fix-G1.1 WATCHOUT "Do not add an explicit lease Release at the zero-hold or interrupt-hold pod release"]: it still holds for the lease. It does not cover the mirror row, which the Sweeper releases (CODE-5 item 1).
FACT: Terminate, delete, and interaction resolution already run on any replica with no lease check, and no inter-replica forward exists, so SPEC-2d needs no production-code change. TEST-0 updates the tier-11 off-holder matrix test that pins the events JSON row and the forwarding-row list (tests/tier11_docs/off_holder_matrix_stated_outcome_test.go). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/route_table.go:49, :58; session_transitions.go; interactions_resolution.go.
FACT: The shipped `eligible` renews any lease held by this replica, bound or not, so the exclusion must come before `eligible` and override `bound` as well as `priorHolder`. It comes before the dead-connection branch too, which would otherwise release the lease of a bound podless row and leave its mirror row unreleased. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:355, :375.
FACT: `coordlease.Store.Upsert` clears `released_at`, so a one-shot mirror release at the suspension release is undone by a sweep that listed the row before the clear and renews after it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordlease/coordlease.go:114.
FACT: `enterResumePending` is guarded on `!holdsPod` of the locked row (CODE-4 item 9), and the release's step 3 is a guarded `Update`. Both run on the primary, so a hold followed by the guarded write and a clear followed by one backlog check leave no podless row with an unnoticed held message outside the residual SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** states. A gateway with no pod binder reports every row as holding a pod, so `sweepSuspended` skips a row only when `holdsPod` is false, and a binderless row keeps the backlog evaluation. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 9, CODE-6 item 4 step 3; pgstore.Get reads the replica (pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:354-357).
FACT: The freshness reaper counts `suspended` as active with no pod check. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/freshness.go:206-215.
DECISION: `awaiting_client_action` keeps 0091's retention. BECAUSE: the state is bounded at 900 s (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:443), the holder's driver restores with no lapse or handoff after a resume call, and 0091's Invariant records that §29.6 **Preconditions.** and the §7.2 durable-inbox rows assume a coordinator there. ALTERNATIVES: drop the lease on entry, which reverses 0091 and adds a 60 s lapse and a handoff before every restore.
DECISION: The terminal write from podless `suspended` takes no new guard. BECAUSE: from podless `suspended` the row reaches only `resume_pending`, `expired`, or a terminal state, and a terminal write that lands in `resume_pending` or `resuming` is settled by the driver's generation compare-and-swap (spec-changes.md edge case "Terminate or cancel racing the driver").
DECISION: The Sweeper releases the mirror row of a podless `suspended` session while it is still the prior holder, and keeps its lease read for such rows. BECAUSE: the release repeats until the lease lapses, so a concurrent re-upsert cannot leave the row live, and it needs no new `Server` option. ALTERNATIVES: skip podless rows before the lease read and release the mirror once in the suspension release, which saves one Redis GET per podless row per replica per sweep and leaves the re-upsert race open (OD-P1).
DECISION: No periodic backlog evaluation of a podless `suspended` session, and no indexes on the `suspended` listings. BECAUSE: the guarded write after each hold and the one check at the release cover every message outside the residual SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** states, and the podless rows then cost no `HeldLen` call. ALTERNATIVES: keep the per-replica evaluation, run it on the gateway leader, or add migration 0183 with two partial indexes (OD-P2, OD-P3).
DECISION: The 300 s `resuming` watchdog stays, and the rebuild bound is filed as RECORDS-1 item 7(m). BECAUSE: the orphan-claim GC reclaims the unreferenced replacement claim after `claimOrphanTimeout`, so raising the watchdog alone does not let a long rebuild finish.
DECISION: A restore with no credential provider assigns no lease (SPEC-1g). The fail-open on a failed tenant or runtime lookup predates 0092 and is filed as RECORDS-1 item 7(n). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/credential_preclaim.go:110-136.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce a forward or an adoption for any podless `suspended` §29.3 row. Interrupt and upload do not admit `suspended`, and resume and messages are store writes under SPEC-3j and SPEC-5l.
WATCHOUT: Do not restore a periodic `HeldLen` probe on podless rows without first showing a message that the guarded write after the hold and the check at the release both miss, outside the residual SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** states.

### [redesign2.apply.1]

DECISION: The podless-session coordination redesign (2026-10-09) was applied from `scratchpad/redesign/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway-redesign-1.md`. Every edit it lists applied, 1 to 42 with the lettered additions; each anchor resolved exactly once, and no edit was skipped. It redesigned three areas: lease holding for a podless `suspended` session (SPEC-2a, new SPEC-2d, CODE-5 item 1), the custody of held messages for such a session (SPEC-5i **Held messages.** and **Backlog resume.**, CODE-6 items 2, 4 step 3a, 7, and 12), and the checkpoint freshness gauge (SPEC-5c, CODE-6 item 15). The reason was that on-demand adoption had no caller, the events JSON form needed no holder, and every replica probed every podless row's backlog every 5 s for the whole suspended lifetime.
DECISION: Deleted by the redesign: on-demand adoption in SPEC-2a; RECORDS-1 item 7(l) as written (the missing on-demand adoption) and its summary **Defects** bullet; the events JSON row's forward in §29.3 and "events JSON" in the forwarding-row list; the periodic backlog evaluation of a podless row; the `HeldLen` call `sweepSuspended` made before any lease check; "writes nothing" for an off-holder hold to a held-pod row; and the lease renewal of a bound podless row. RECORDS-1 item 7 now carries (l) unindexed listings, (m) the rebuild bound, (n) the credential lookup fail-open, and (o) the §10.4 replay buffer.
DECISION: Reconciliation beyond the redesign's own edits: the CODE-5 item 3 comments name the podless exclusion; the summary gains three watch-outs (the skip placement before `eligible` and the dead-connection branch, the primary-side ordering of the hold write and the release check, and the `HoldsPod` binderless case); checklist S2 names the §29.3 rows and S6 depends on S2 as well as S5; the Standing context **Custody home.** entry names the guarded write after a podless hold.
OPEN: OD-P1 (mirror release in the Sweeper, default, or once in the suspension release), OD-P2 (no indexes on the `suspended` listings, default, or migration 0183), OD-P3 (no periodic backlog evaluation of podless rows, default, or per-replica or leader evaluation), and OD-P4 (file the §10.4 and §15.1 replay-buffer inconsistency as RECORDS-1 item 7(o), default, or rewrite the §15.1 cell in SPEC-2d). The staged text applies each default; the summary's open-decisions section lists them.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: on-demand adoption of a podless `suspended` session before a §29.3 row that needs a coordinator. No inter-replica forward exists to call it, terminate, delete, and resolution already run on any replica with no lease check, and the events JSON form reads relay history on any replica. Do not re-add an adoption or a forward for a podless row; SPEC-2d states the rows.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the periodic backlog evaluation of every podless `suspended` row on every replica every 5 s. It cost one `HeldLen` per row per replica per tick over the 7-day population. The guarded `enterResumePending` after each hold and the one backlog check at the release cover every message outside the residual SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** states (OD-P3).
WATCHOUT: Replaced: lease renewal of a bound podless row and the one-shot mirror release in the suspension release (Specification 2, step 5 with `Options.CoordinationMirror`). `coordlease.Store.Upsert` clears `released_at`, so a sweep that listed the row before the clear re-upserts the mirror after a one-shot release. The Sweeper releases the mirror on every sweep while it is the prior holder (OD-P1).
WATCHOUT: Replaced: migration 0183 with `ListFilter.SuspendedAtOrBefore` and `ListFilter.SuspendedPodHeld`. The Sweeper, the freshness reaper, and the other watchdog sweeps still list unindexed, and podless rows no longer need backlog Redis work. RECORDS-1 item 7(l) files the listings (OD-P2).

### [mechanism.credential-leases.1]

CORRECTS [archive FACT: "The 'resumed session runs with no leases' gap predates 0092; do not file it against the suspension release."]: the gap is real and predates 0092. The owner chose option A on 2026-10-09, and 0092 stages the fix in SPEC-1g and CODE-4 item 20, so it is fixed here and not filed. CORRECTS [archive WATCHOUT: "the checkpoint branch mints no lease"]: after CODE-4 item 20 the checkpoint branch mints, so the lost-commit release case runs on both branches and on both pool kinds.
FACT: The adapter admits AssignCredentials then Resume under one token and refuses AssignCredentials after Resume. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/credentials.go:79-82; pkg/adapter/slot.go:375-386; pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:116.
FACT: The replacement-pod sequence "AssignCredentials (new lease) → Resume" is in §4.7.8. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:864.
FACT (closes the UNVERIFIED on the suspension release's session-wide revoke): confirmed. PodExecutor.Release unbinds and Binder.Release calls releaseCredentials first, while releaseEarlierBinding alone holds slotAccountLocks. CODE-6 item 4 step 4 now takes the lock. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:257-271; release.go:60-66; resume_rebind.go:165-177.
FACT: the failure funnel releases the reported binding outside slotAccountLocks on an exclusive pool, and after unlock() on a slot pool, so a same-replica driver attempt after its resume_pending commit can lose its new leases to that release. CODE-4 item 22 holds the lock on both pool kinds through releaseReportedBinding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:205-212, :236-239, :285.
FACT (closes archive UNVERIFIED "whether the suspension release needs a lease-preserving release"): it does not. §6.1 requires a fresh assignment per dispatch, and the restore now assigns one. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:26.
DECISION: every failure from the credential assignment onward goes through failResume, which compensates — BECAUSE AssignCredentials can create the registry entry, and materializeSlot already compensates every stage failure, including a minting failure. ### [reconcile.mechanisms.1]

DECISION: the Open section's entries are disposed of as follows on 2026-10-09.
- OD-2, OD-3, OD-B, and OD-R1: answered by the owner; Decisions 20 to 23.
- `slotRetries` and `maxRetries` composition: CODE-3 item 7.
- §6.2 lost interrupt context for an idle suspension: SPEC-5d2.
- `ResumeSuspended` off-holder on a held pod: closed. Its only callers run after a message is held (`ResumeBacklog`, and the MCP path after `HoldMessage` returns `queued` or `dropped`), so the coordinating replica's backlog resume fires.
- `delivered_message_ids`: closed; RECORDS-1 item 7(i) files it.
- DLQ enqueue failure with no metric: closed. The client receives the error and keeps its message, and the behavior predates 0092 (messages_inbox.go:57).
- Redis DLQ key outside §12.8 erasure: RECORDS-1 item 7(k).
- Periodic and drain checkpoints without the 60 s deadline: closed; predates 0092, and CODE-6 item 4.2 bounds its own call.
- `isTransientPodClaimError` and `errResumeHoldStale`: CODE-4 item 8 deletes them.
- `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` on a superseded attempt or a lost commit: CODE-4 items 3.6 and 5.3.
- Details key for the coordinating replica: CODE-4 item 8 drops `coordinatingReplica`.
- Comments crediting `bumpRecoveryGeneration`: CODE-2 item 1.
- Corrupt payload at the DLQ head: CODE-4 item 6.
- Concurrent-pool lost-ack residuals: Edge cases, **Concurrent-pool counter after an ambiguous commit.**
- Landed commit not `running` runs no tail: CODE-4 item 5.3 runs the counter and audit row.
- `sweepSuspended` non-sentinel `Update` error: CODE-6 item 2.1.
- `PurgeRoot` SCAN on a Redis Cluster client: CODE-6 item 14 `scanTree`.
- session-lifecycle.md `/resume` preconditions row: DOCS-1b.
- CH-FENCE "This channel is what changes the holder": SPEC-2c item 8.
- Child `TaskResult`s while the parent is podless `suspended`: closed. They are built from the child's row (lifecycle.go:241, taskrecord.go), so the parent's pod holds none.
- `gateway.maxClientIdleSeconds`: CODE-6 item 11 deletes it; the spec binds the bound only as `sessionPolicy.maxClientIdleSeconds`.
- Tier-5 `stream_failure_kind_test.go`: tests that land with CODE-4.
- Suspension release revoking a resume attempt's leases: [mechanism.credential-leases.1].
- `TestReleaseSuspendedPodRunsOffTheCaller` and the flight lock: closed. The test injects a fake `SuspensionCheckpointer`, and the in-flight set covers the second call.
- `deliverBuffered` with no messaging coordinator, and REST/MCP parity: CODE-4 item 6; parity holds through `ErrMessagingUnavailable`.
- `fenceResumedPod` relinquish after the commit: CODE-4 item 5.4.
- Nil `deps.Delivery`: CODE-6 item 7 and its wiring test.
- `ErrSessionSuspended` on a delegation child's first `Send` and the single-shot translators: closed; CODE-6 item 5.4 states and accepts the outcome.
- Watchdog constant comments: CODE-6 item 9.
- Claim-register "Session inbox redelivery" row: RECORDS-1 item 8.
- `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 4 `releaseExecutor` error: CODE-6 item 4 step 4.
- Failure-funnel release revoking a resume attempt's leases: CODE-4 item 22.
- Build order of `PrepareResult.MintedLeaseIDs` and `SessionDelivery`: CODE-3 item 3 adds the field; the delegate-handler delivery is CODE-6 item 7.

### [redesign1.apply.1]

DECISION: Redesign 1 (2026-10-09) was applied from `scratchpad/redesign/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway-redesign-1.md`. Every edit it lists applied; its anchors all resolved once, and no edit was skipped. It redesigned four areas: credential leases across suspend and resume (owner option A: SPEC-1g, SPEC-3q, CODE-4 items 20 and 22, CODE-6 item 4 step 4), the release after the claim and its retry accounting (CODE-3 items 2 to 4 and 7, CODE-4 items 3.6, 5.3, and 5.4), the owner answers to OD-2, OD-3, OD-B, and OD-R1 (Decisions 20 to 23, SPEC-1e item 5, SPEC-5i, SPEC-5z item 10, CODE-2 item 5, CODE-4 item 21, CODE-6 item 7), and the docs split (DOCS-1a keeps the API, error-catalog, and SDK-example pages; DOCS-1b owns every other page whole).
DECISION: Deleted by the redesign: the `podsession.ErrOnClaimed` sentinel and its test; `isTransientPodClaimError`, `errResumeHoldStale`, and their tests; the `coordinatingReplica` refusal details key; the chart value `gateway.maxClientIdleSeconds`; the `failResume` doc paragraph "It releases no §4.9 credential lease."; the session-wide release on the `Bind` reconnect and launch failures; the summary's open-decision list; and every entry of the Open section.
DECISION: Reconciliation beyond the redesign's own edits: the DOCS-1a Content paragraph drops the retry-accounting sentence, which N25 moved to DOCS-1b; the lease-preserving release moved from Decision 8 to Non-goals; Decision 22 drops its comparison with the backlog resume; Decision 2 says the driver adds no state edge; the summary gains two watch-outs (the `slotAccountLocks` ordering of the suspension and funnel releases, and the attempt-scoped `Bind` release); the checklist S1 and S3 lines and the CODE-3 deliverable-index line name the new content. CODE-4 item 5.4 keeps its stamp-removal sentences in the step lead, ahead of the three outcome bullets N12 adds.
OPEN: Decision 23 (OD-R1) stages the delivery at the pre-running commit, which the summary's OD-R1 entry listed as the alternative; its default left the row unbuilt. The redesign took the answer from its Specification 2, and its section 4 item 2 asked the orchestrator to confirm Decisions 20 to 23 against the owner's message. Decisions 20 (the OD-2 recommendation), 21, and 22 match the summary's recommendation or default.
OPEN: RECORDS-1 item 7(k) files the Redis DLQ and inbox keys outside §12.8 user erasure (redesign section 4 item 1, default). The alternative stages a §12.8 spec edit and an erasure code step in this proposal.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: a restore that assigns no credential lease. `Binder.Resume` minted none while the suspension release and `releaseEarlierBinding` revoked every lease, so a checkpoint-restored runtime started with an empty credential file. It lost to §6.1 and §4.7.8, which require a fresh assignment before `Resume`. Do not drop `AssignCredentials` from `Binder.Resume` or move it after `cl.Resume`.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the `podsession.ErrOnClaimed` sentinel. `runWithQueue` re-enters only on `isExhaustion` and `applySlotRetryPolicy` retries only a `*SlotBindError`, so a hook error stops both loops unwrapped. Do not re-add a sentinel for the hook error.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the session-wide `ReleaseSession` on the `Bind` reconnect and launch failures. It revokes a lease another attempt minted for the same session. The `attemptScoped` request releases the attempt's own `PrepareResult.MintedLeaseIDs`; the decomposed `/finalize` and `/start` path keeps the session-wide release.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the unlocked suspension release (CODE-6 item 4 step 4) and the funnel's release after `unlock()`. Each let a same-replica resume assign leases that the later `releaseCredentials` revoked. Both now hold `slotAccountLocks` across `releaseExecutor`; do not narrow the funnel lock back to slot bindings.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the `coordinatingReplica` refusal details key and the chart value `gateway.maxClientIdleSeconds`. Neither has a spec basis. Do not re-add either.

### [non-spec.18.fix-G1.1]
FACT: `Binder.connect` claims and then runs `resolveSandbox`, `DialAdapter`, and `NegotiateVersion` before returning; a handshake failure closes the client and deletes no claim. `Binder.Bind` reaches it through `Claim`; `connectSlot` wraps the same steps in `SlotBindError` after `ClaimSlot`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/claimconnect.go (connect), bindclaim.go (Claim), binder.go (Bind), slotbinder.go (connectSlot)
DECISION: CODE-3 item 2 splits `connect` into a claim half and a handshake half and invokes the hook between them (Resume: after `reserveResumeSlot`; BindSlot: after `ClaimSlot`, before `resolveSandbox`); item 3 widens the release to post-hook resolve/dial/handshake failures — BECAUSE SPEC-1b item 2 says "before the first RPC to the pod", and a post-hook handshake failure then takes CODE-4 item 3.7 (reported with G, spends a retry) instead of leaking a claim per backoff as a "pre-claim" failure — ALTERNATIVES: rewording SPEC-1b to "entry-touching RPC" (reopens spec loop, broken-adapter pool drains one pod per backoff for the whole window); hook before `reserveResumeSlot` (hook-error DeleteClaim can evict a sibling slot, the round-1 defect); release in `Claim` only when the hook is non-nil (branch with no spec basis).
WATCHOUT: do not add a release inside the shared `connect` for every path; the Resume slot path must release through `releaseResumeSlot` (reservation only), never `DeleteClaim`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go (releaseResumeSlot)
WATCHOUT: only the hook error wraps `ErrOnClaimed`; a post-hook handshake failure must stay retryable so BindSlot's `applySlotRetryPolicy` retries it, and CODE-4 item 3.4's per-attempt no-op flag covers the second hook call.

### [non-spec.18.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 moves the OnClaimed hook to the claim boundary (after the claim and any slot reservation, before resolveSandbox/DialAdapter/NegotiateVersion) by splitting `connect` into a claim half and a handshake half; item 3's release trigger widens to "a hook error, or a resolve, dial, or handshake failure after the hook" with the same per-path helpers, and the cl.Close steps go — BECAUSE SPEC-1b item 2 ("before the first RPC to the pod") is the home and needs no edit; a post-hook handshake failure then spends a retry through item 3.7, bounding pod loss by maxRetries — ALTERNATIVES: reword SPEC-1b to "first entry-touching RPC" and only release the claim on connect failure (spec loop closed; a handshake failure would stay "pre-claim", spending no retry, so a broken-adapter pool drains one pod per backoff for the whole window); hook inside the claim half before reserveResumeSlot (a concurrent start can reserve a sibling slot between claim CREATE and reservation, so the hook-error DeleteClaim would evict siblings — the round-1 defect).
DECISION: the handshake-failure release in `Claim` is unconditional, so create (nil hook) also deletes the claim on a failed handshake — BECAUSE conditioning on a non-nil hook is a branch with no spec basis, and DeleteClaim is the gateway's drain (reclaim.go `drain`), so the broken pod is not returned to idle.
FACT: `connect` (claimconnect.go) has two callers, `Claim` (bindclaim.go, which `Bind` and create use) and `Binder.Resume`; `connectSlot` has two, `BindSlot` and the slot claim at slotbinder.go:163. A connect handshake failure today closes the client and deletes no claim on either exclusive path; connectSlot's handshake failures are SlotBindErrors the slot retry policy releases. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/claimconnect.go:60-112, slotbinder.go:635-694
FACT: DeleteClaim is the gateway's reclaim (drain) action; the WPC projects the occupancy phase on the claim DELETE. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:87-100
WATCHOUT: `reserveResumeSlot` takes `sb.Name` today; after the split it takes the claimed sandbox name before `resolveSandbox`. `Claim`'s PodClaim phase timing will include the hook's store write on Bind. EVIDENCE: resume.go:129-145, bindclaim.go:56-74
OPEN: a `reserveResumeSlot` failure on Resume is after the claim and before the hook, so CODE-4 item 3.5 calls it pre-claim and nothing releases the claim (pre-existing; not this finding). A later round should decide whether it is a separate finding.

### [non-spec.18.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: `Binder.connect` issues a pod RPC (`cl.NegotiateVersion`) after the claim and before returning, and on a resolve, dial, or negotiate failure it only closes the connection; nothing deletes the claim, so the claimed pod waits for the orphan-claim GC (`--claim-orphan-timeout`, default 5m). `Binder.Resume` and `Binder.Claim` (the `Bind` path) both run `connect` before the CODE-3 hook. `connectSlot` returns a `SlotBindError` instead, which the slot retry policy releases. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/claimconnect.go:60-101, resume.go:129-145, bindclaim.go:58-63, slotbinder.go:672-694, cmd/lenny-controller/flags.go:177
WATCHOUT: The staged spec (SPEC-1b item 2) and the summary say `resuming` is written "before the first RPC to the pod". CODE-3 item 2 and its tests place the hook after the version handshake ("first entry-touching RPC"). So a handshake failure on the replacement pod becomes a CODE-4 item 3.5 pre-claim failure: no write, no retry spent, and a leaked claim on every driver backoff cycle. Filed this round. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:68, non-spec-changes.md:46-49, :69, :265
FACT: The traced message flows all reach delivery-and-removal or `message_expired`: idle suspension, the zero-hold release (sub-item 2 runs before 3 in the same tick, and the release guard fails once the row goes `running`), podless and held-pod resume on and off the coordinator, the commit, and level-triggered delivery. Of the custody, OD-1 anchor, and generation-increment rules, I found no copy beyond its stated home.

### [non-spec.18.review-performance.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings in non-spec round 18. BECAUSE the only r16-to-r18 change moves the post-hook slot-retry test from the CODE-3 bullet to the CODE-4 driver bullet, which adds no write, no store dependency, and no watch. The new placement agrees with CODE-4 item 3.4, where the per-attempt flag turns a second hook call into a no-op that returns nil. Re-tracing the remaining surfaces turned up nothing new: the per-Send `StampSync` Postgres write (OD-A, human-resolved), the `sweepSuspended` per-row `HeldLen` and the 7-day population (refuted as a design choice), `RearmTree` SCAN cost (refuted), and the `HeldLen` error under a Redis reset, which skips the row for the tick and leaves the pod held. The proposal adds no etcd write. ALTERNATIVES: refiling the Redis-reset loss of DLQ-held messages for a `suspended` session. Rejected because the §12.4 failure table has no DLQ row, the shipped design already holds `queued` messages in the same Redis key, and SPEC-5i deliberately states no at-least-once sentence (review-log.md:143).
USEFUL [Standing context "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population" and "Refuted: store-outage degradation"]: these two entries cover most of this lens's candidate findings. Read them first.

TerminalDLQTTL (60 s) has no caller. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:274; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:379; pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/coordinator.go:127; pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:23-28
FACT: DefaultDLQTTL is 900 s. A held message whose podless resume does not commit within 900 s of acceptance expires with `dlq_ttl_expired` at the first `resume_pending`/`resuming` sweep. This is spec-stated in SPEC-5i, so it is not a defect. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:23
FACT: `handleInterrupt` is the only caller of `transitionInterrupt`, and it stamps SuspendedAt and SuspendedReason. After CODE-6 item 1 removes the MCP write (mcptools_register.go:553), no writer of `suspended` leaves SuspendedAt zero. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go:79-88; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go:119
WATCHOUT: Watchdog expiries, including the new sweepSuspended lifetime expiry, run on every replica. `OnSessionTerminal` → `drainMessagingOnTerminal` drains the winning replica's MemoryInbox and releases through that replica's executor. When a non-coordinator wins, the coordinator's in-memory inbox entries are not drained and the pod is reclaimed only by the orphan GC. This predates 0092 (sweepMaxAge does the same), so it is not filed against this proposal. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:302; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messaging.go:75-80; pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/coordinator.go:196

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: A failure the resume driver reports after its `resume_pending → resuming` write carries reason `setup_command_failed` for a setup-command failure (step 3e) and `runtime_crash` otherwise; the unchanged §7.3 step 2 classifier picks the §6.2 edge. Home is SPEC-1b **Resume driver.** item 3 — BECAUSE applied §7.3 step 2 makes an unlisted reason non-retryable (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md "A failure whose reason is on neither list is non-retryable"), so a credential mint or claim-retry failure after the claim would have gone straight to awaiting_client_action; `runtime_crash` reuses the §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH precedent and matches CODE-4 item 3.7 as staged — ALTERNATIVES: a new retryable reason `resume_failed` (new client-visible policy surface); relabelling §6.2 only (an unlisted reason stays non-retryable); a cause list with exception clause in §6.2 (longer, leaves reason unstated).
DECISION: §6.2 **`resuming` failure transitions:** first bullet relabelled "**Retryable failure during `resuming`** (§7.3 classifier)", and its non-retryable examples become "a setup-command failure during a snapshotless rebuild" (SPEC-1c sub-edit). Both §7.2 `resuming` lines use retryable/non-retryable vocabulary with the retries-exhausted branch on the watchdog (SPEC-1e items 5 and 6) — BECAUSE the old awaiting line listed the watchdog unconditionally, contradicting the inserted resume_pending line and §6.2's retry-count branching.
WATCHOUT: Do not restore "workspace checkpoint corrupt" or "policy rejection" as non-retryable examples in §6.2's resuming bullet; under item 3 the driver reports both as `runtime_crash`, which is retryable. "policy rejection" elsewhere in spec/06 (running → failed, Non-retryable failures bullets) is a different context and stays. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:151,176,242
WATCHOUT: Do not re-add a cause list ("whatever its cause, including a failed connection or version handshake ... pool exhaustion") to **Retry accounting.**, or an assignment-failure route sentence to SPEC-1g; item 3 of **Resume driver.** is the one home of post-claim classification. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1b, SPEC-1g
FACT: SPEC-1e item 6 edits the same §7.2 line item 5 uses as its insertion anchor; apply item 5 before item 6 or match on "resuming → awaiting_client_action". — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:203

A corrupt checkpoint is therefore retried up to maxRetries; accepted as bounded.

### [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix summary]: "the CODE-2 and CODE-3 item 7 pointers to Retry accounting are still true" was wrong for CODE-3 item 7's third bullet, which said every post-hook failure spends a retry. After SPEC-1b item 3 routes a post-claim `setup_command_failed` to `awaiting_client_action` (non-retryable under §7.3), that report spends nothing; Retry accounting advances `retryCount` only on a move into `resume_pending`.
DECISION: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 item 7 third bullet now reads "every failure is reported once as CODE-4 item 3.7 states; SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.** states which reports spend a retry." — BECAUSE a pure pointer cannot drift when Retry accounting changes again — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "a `runtime_crash` report spends a retry" restates the rule and is also false when retries are exhausted (the classifier sends that report to `awaiting_client_action`).
FACT: Citer sweep over "spends a retry", "Retry accounting", and `setup_command_failed` in the summary, checklist, and non-spec-changes found no other site the narrowed rule falsifies. The CODE-4 tier-1 list already asserts the setup `FailedPrecondition` landing in `awaiting_client_action` and asserts `RetryCount + 1` only for the `runtime_crash` drain case. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md tier-1 driver bullet
WATCHOUT: CODE-4 item 3.6 store-error bullet ("`sweepResuming` ends it after `MaxResumingSeconds`, spending one retry") omits the exhausted-retries exit, which spends nothing. Neither fix touched it, so it is left for the non-spec loop. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 3.6

DECISION: filed one finding: the accepted "Session-wide lease release on a replica that lost the lease" residual (non-spec Edge cases) lands in no spec text and is absent from the spec-changes Edge cases list. ALTERNATIVES: a docs-only remedy is out of scope for the spec loop.
FACT: credential trace for running → idle-suspended → released → resumed: the zero-hold release (SPEC-5b step 2, CODE-6 item 4 step 4) returns every lease via releaseCredentials; the driver re-runs the §4.9 pre-claim check before the claim (SPEC-1g) and assigns new leases after the resuming write, before Resume, so §4.9 SPIFFE binding (spec/04:1623-1626) records the replacement pod. Same-replica ordering is by slotAccountLocks only. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:182, :428
FACT: §4.9 Pre-Claim Credential Availability Check (spec/04:1324-1328) is scoped to §7.1 step 4 and the create→finalize window; SPEC-1g gives the resume path its own outcome (claim failure under Resume driver item 1), so no contradiction.
WATCHOUT: docs-lens findings in this spec loop must have a spec remedy; every "docs page not updated" item belongs to the non-spec loop (Standing context "Refuted: docs-mirror findings").
