### [spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The redesign-4 staging (SPEC-1g credential leases, SPEC-3q) still adds no CRD field, status write, finalizer, webhook, or controller on a request path. Credential leases live in TokenStore with a per-lease TTL (`leaseTTLSeconds`), and SPIFFE binding records the issuing pod's URI at `AssignCredentials` time, so a lease assigned to the replacement pod is bound to that pod. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1253, :1623-1626; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:26

USEFUL [archive FACT on Binder.Resume claiming a fresh idle pod]: SPEC-1b's statement that "No session's `pod_assignment` names that pod" holds on concurrent pools as well, because the replacement claim's binding-state transition is fresh. Without this fact, a concurrent-pod orphan-GC finding looks plausible, so do not refile it. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517
WATCHOUT: A `resuming` attempt that crashes leaks the leases it assigned (SPEC-1g says "releases the leases it assigned", which a crashed replica cannot do). The lease TTL bounds the leak. This is a credential-pool capacity point and not a Kubernetes-idiom point; check it under a security or capacity lens before filing. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1253

### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: Traced credential leases through running → idle-suspended (hold zero) → release → message → resume_pending → driver. The spec staging is consistent: the release returns the pod's leases (§7.1 step 23), SPEC-1g runs the pre-claim check before the claim and AssignCredentials after the entry write and before Resume (matches §4.7.8 and the §4.7.1 registry rules: AssignCredentials creates and stamps the entry under the same bind_attempt token as Resume), and the new lease records the replacement pod's SPIFFE URI under §4.9 SPIFFE-binding item 1. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:695, :708-710, :864; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:54
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1e item 5 adds `resuming → resume_pending (... resuming watchdog fires, retryCount < maxRetries)` directly above the unedited line `resuming → awaiting_client_action (re-attach fails after retries exhausted, resuming watchdog fires, or non-retryable error ...)`, which carries no retry qualifier on the watchdog. Filed. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:203; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241
WATCHOUT: Nothing in the spec classifies a credential-assignment failure after the claim as retryable. §7.3 step 2 treats a reason on neither list as non-retryable, and §6.2 lists "policy rejection" as non-retryable, which matches CREDENTIAL_POOL_EXHAUSTED's POLICY category. Meanwhile CODE-4 item 3.7 reports every post-claim error as `runtime_crash`. Filed against SPEC-1g. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:393, :411; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:242; spec/04_system-components.md:1326; non-spec-changes.md:86
FACT: The §10.1.2 step 1 0-row path ("discard its lease claim, and restart the handoff from lease acquisition") is what keeps the other race order safe, when the attempt's entry write lands before the acquirer's CAS. On the retry, the acquirer's step 0 re-read finds `resuming`, and SPEC-2a does not adopt that state. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:38
FACT: The retention and settlement rules for coordination_generation, held-message custody, and the resuming watchdog are each stated in full only once (SPEC-1b, SPEC-5i, and §6.2 respectively). The other sites are pointers or one-clause consequences. Not filed.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings in redesign-4 spec round 1. BECAUSE the redesign-4 additions (SPEC-1g credential assignment on the restore, the Retry accounting widening, SPEC-1e item 5, SPEC-2c item 8, SPEC-3q, SPEC-5d2, the podless-miss `resume_pending` in the path 6 lead, the pre-running delivery point, and SPEC-5z item 10) add no etcd write and no new watch. The only new per-unit store work is one Token Service assignment per resume and one DLQ peek per commit to `running` from a pre-running state (at most 200/s at Tier 3). Neither approaches a stated budget. — ALTERNATIVES: (1) Warm-pool claim demand from idle-suspension resumes. Rejected: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1040 "Expected claim rate" is generic and operator-measured, and the bound depends on the workload. (2) etcd claim churn per suspend/resume cycle. Rejected: the bound is about 10,000/900 s ≈ 11 extra claims/s × about 5 writes, roughly 60 writes/s, against the ~800/s Tier 3 estimate (spec/04_system-components.md:473). (3) Token Service outage blocking the resume of idle-released sessions. Rejected: the proposal's Edge cases accept it explicitly ("Pre-claim failure"), and it follows from the owner's option A.
FACT: Credential leases end to end. Idle suspension (hold zero) releases the pod, and §7.1 step 23 returns lease L1. The driver runs the pre-claim availability check (spec/04_system-components.md:1326), claims P2, writes `resuming` (generation incremented), handshakes, and calls AssignCredentials (L2) → Resume (spec/04_system-components.md:864). At AssignCredentials, L2 is recorded against P2's SPIFFE URI (spec/04_system-components.md:1624), so the resumed runtime can reach its provider through the proxy. EVIDENCE: as cited.
WATCHOUT: A driver attempt whose replica crashes after AssignCredentials and before the commit leaks its leases until lease TTL (default 3600 s, spec/04_system-components.md:1257 table). Those leases count against the credential's `maxConcurrentSessions`. The create path has the same leak. Do not file it as a 0092 regression unless the leak reaches a stated budget. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1g last sentence.
USEFUL [Standing context "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population" and "Refuted: store-outage degradation"]: these entries covered the lease, HeldLen, StampSync-failover, and DLQ-Redis-reset candidates again.

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in redesign-4 spec round 1 — BECAUSE tracing credential leases (running → idle-suspended hold zero → checkpoint, conditional binding clear, release returning L1 per §7.1 step 23 → message → DLQ enqueue → suspended → resume_pending → driver pre-claim check → claim P2 → resuming write (generation +1) → AssignCredentials L2 recorded against P2's SPIFFE URI → Resume → commit → held delivery) found no loss, double-apply, or stall that is new, unaccepted, and fixable in the staged spec. Crash leaks (an attempt's leases after AssignCredentials, a stopped attempt's pod) are bounded by lease TTL and the claim-orphan GC respectively — ALTERNATIVES: filing the crashed-attempt lease leak (rejected: TTL-bounded, same as the create path; see spec.1.review-performance WATCHOUT); filing the failed-re-read answer in **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** as a duplicate source (rejected: a removal that finds the entry still does not prove non-delivery, so no spec wording closes it, and it needs a Postgres failure on one replica only).
FACT: The resume window (§7.3 step 3c), the resuming watchdog (300 s, §6.2), the suspended lifetime, and the interrupt hold are all anchored on row fields, so each survives a coordinator restart; the driver's pre-claim backoff is jittered in CODE-4 item 1.5 and flag item 12. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241; non-spec-changes.md:81, :125
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-6 item 5.2/5.3 `TurnInFlight` reads only the local executor's `sending` counter and turn token, so after a coordinator handoff (a rolling update included) the adopter reports no turn in flight while the runtime may still be processing a message the previous coordinator delivered; `SuspendIdle` can then suspend and checkpoint a session mid-turn when no qualifying event arrived for `maxClientIdleSeconds`. The spec predicate (SPEC-5f "no message delivered to it is still being processed") is correct and implementable from the adapter's `ready_for_input` (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:327, :329); the code approximation is what is wrong after handoff. A non-spec reviewer should decide whether this meets the bar. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:187-188
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1b's "No session's `pod_assignment` names that pod, so ... drains it" assumes no sibling joined the attempt's freshly claimed concurrent pod; the archive records that a concurrent start can reserve a sibling slot on it (review-log-archive.md:1375), and then the GC predicate (spec/04_system-components.md:517 "whose pod no active session references") never fires for the stopped attempt's slot. spec.1.review-kubernetes said not to refile and the archive (review-log-archive.md:2414) judged the slot leak pre-existing on every release path; I did not file it. Refile only with a concrete reclaimer that the spec should name.
USEFUL [spec.1.review-performance WATCHOUT on the crashed-attempt lease leak]: saved re-deriving the lease TTL bound.
USEFUL [Standing context "Refuted: store-outage degradation" and "Refuted: GC races"]: covered the DLQ-on-Redis-loss and binding-clear-before-release candidates again.

### [spec.1.review-security.1]

FACT: Credential leases traced end to end for running → idle-suspended → released → message-resumed. The release revokes the pod's leases (SPEC-5b, SPEC-1g citing §7.1 step 23); the driver runs the §4.9 pre-claim check before the claim, then assigns new leases after the entry write and before `Resume` (SPEC-1g, SPEC-3q). §4.9 records the issuing pod's SPIFFE URI at `AssignCredentials` time, so the new leases bind to the replacement pod and no lease stays bound to the released pod. The multi-tenant deliveryMode/isolation admission check is stated for "every path that mints a credential lease", so it covers the restore without a staged edit. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1623-1628, :864, :1324-1326; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:26.
FACT: The Standard/Basic credential-rotation restart (§4.7.8, §4.9 Fallback Flow step 7) is not modelled in the session state machine and has no implementation in pkg/gateway, so it does not conflict with SPEC-1b "no other component writes resume_pending → resuming". EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1517, :864.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1g says "one lease per provider the resolution names", while §4.9 **Intersection at session creation.** skips providers that have no available credential at creation. Whether a partial assignment on resume fails the attempt is unstated. This is availability semantics rather than security, so it is not filed under this lens. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1434.
WATCHOUT: The Redis DLQ and inbox keys are not in the §12.8 DeleteByUser order (12_storage-architecture.md:840-852). With the durable-inbox EXPIRE deleted (OD-R2, owner-settled), the inbox key of an erased user's session has no expiry. It is recorded as RECORDS-1 7(k) with the default applied. Do not refile it as a spec finding unless the owner reopens OD-R2.

### [spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The credential trace holds end to end in the staged spec. Idle suspension releases the pod, and SPEC-1g says the release returns the session's leases (§7.1 step 23). The driver resolves pools and runs the pre-claim check before the claim, so a failure there writes nothing (item 1). AssignCredentials runs after the resuming write and before Resume, and it mints leases whose SPIFFE binding is the replacement pod's (§4.9 SPIFFE-binding item 1 records the issuing pod at AssignCredentials time). Any failure after the claim is reported as runtime_crash and classified at §7.3 step 2 (SPEC-1b item 3). EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1624, spec-changes.md:69, :153
FACT: When a §10.1.2 step 1 CAS fails because a resume-driver bump landed between step 0 and step 1, the adopter discards its lease claim and restarts from lease acquisition (§10.1.2 step 1). The existing 0-row path covers this, and on the restart step 0 finds `resuming`, which SPEC-2a does not adopt. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:37
WATCHOUT: The checkpoint-manifest resume selection filters on MAX(coordination_generation). Resume-driver bumps keep that selection correct because the bumps are monotonic, so do not file it as broken by the driver's increment. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:171
WATCHOUT: §6.2 line 176 ("policy rejections are not retried") concerns the running-session pod-crash path. SPEC-1c removing "policy rejection" from the `resuming` non-retryable example does not contradict it. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:176, :242

DECISION: returned no findings in redesign-4 spec round 3 under the Kubernetes-idiom lens — BECAUSE the staged spec writes no CRD spec or status field, adds no condition, finalizer, or webhook rule, and keeps every new coordination (resume driver, held-message custody, idle suspension, coordination_generation) in Postgres and Redis; the only Kubernetes touchpoints are the gateway-owned SandboxClaim (create, binding-state patch, release) and the WarmPoolController's level-triggered orphan GC, both unchanged in ownership — ALTERNATIVES: filing the 300s resuming watchdog vs 5m claimOrphanTimeout overlap (rejected: accepted outcome stated in SPEC-1b and the orphan-claim DECISION).
FACT: SandboxClaim spec and status are gateway-owned (single writer), and the orphan GC is WarmPoolController-leader-only and keys liveness on Postgres pod_assignment, so a pre-commit resume pod is reclaimable by GC without any gateway-to-controller status signal. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:619, :515, :517

### [spec.3.review-performance.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings in redesign-4 spec round 3. BECAUSE the r1-to-r3 diff (SPEC-1b item 3 failure-reason classification, the SPEC-1c bullet relabel and example swap, SPEC-1e item 6, SPEC-1g2) adds no store write, watch, or per-unit work. It only reclassifies post-claim failures, and the retry budget bounds each of them. ALTERNATIVES: (1) A Token Service outage after the claim spends a retry and a warm pod for each attempt. Rejected: pool lease minting goes through the gateway's in-process `Credentials.AssignProto` (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/credentiallease.go:99), and the real keys sit in the gateway's Token Service cache (spec/04_system-components.md:1588). So it is speculative that an outage reaches the post-claim mint at all. (2) A credential exhaustion between the pre-claim check and the assignment spends a retry. Rejected: the window is only the claim plus the handshake, so the event is rare and bounded by maxRetries. (3) A corrupt checkpoint is now reported as `runtime_crash` and retried, which costs up to maxRetries+1 checkpoint downloads. Rejected: the cost is bounded by a small constant factor.
FACT: `assignCredentials` fails the whole attempt when any one provider's `AssignProto` fails (credentiallease.go:98-106), while §4.9 "Intersection at session creation" says providers without an available credential are skipped (spec/04_system-components.md:1434). This mismatch predates 0092 and also affects finalize. SPEC-1g's "one lease per provider the resolution names" follows the code. Do not file it against 0092. EVIDENCE: as cited.
FACT: The §4.9 pre-claim check reads pool utilization, cooldown, and health only. It does not consult the §4.3 Token Service circuit breaker. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1326, :222-228.
USEFUL [Standing context "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population" and "Refuted: store-outage degradation"; spec.1.review-performance.1 redesign-4 alternatives]: these covered the claim-rate, etcd-churn, StampSync-failover, and lease-leak candidates again, so none of them was re-derived.

### [spec.3.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability finding in the staged spec edits this round — BECAUSE the round-2 changes (SPEC-1b item 3 failure reasons plus classifier, SPEC-1c bullet relabel, SPEC-1e item 6, SPEC-1g2) keep the resuming exits single-incarnation, the retry spend bounded by maxRetries, and every crash point between claim, entry write, AssignCredentials, and commit covered by the incarnation guard, the watchdog, or the orphan-claim GC — ALTERNATIVES: filing post-claim Token Service or credential exhaustion spending a retry (rejected: a design choice, and the pre-claim check catches an open breaker); filing leaked leases of a crashed attempt (rejected: the same class as a finalize crash, bounded by lease expiresAt, spec/04_system-components.md:1253)

### [spec.3.review-security.1]
FACT: No current spec sentence says a suspension release (the §6.2 graceful release) returns the session's credential leases. §6.1 revokes a lease only when the session "completes or fails" or "the runtime exits", §7.1 step 23 is the session-end release, and §5.2's recycle purge removes only the pod-side credential file. The gateway-side revocation is the proxy-mode control, because SPIFFE binding is per pod and a recycled pod keeps its SPIFFE URI. Owner Decision 8 has no spec home until SPEC-5b step 2 states it. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:153, spec-changes.md:431

### [spec.5.review-docs-alignment.1]
DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE every docs-lens candidate either has its remedy in docs/ (out of this loop's scope) or its accepted outcome already lands in staged spec text (SPEC-1b items 1 to 3, SPEC-1g, SPEC-2a, SPEC-5f, SPEC-5o) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the §7.3 "awaiting_client_action semantics: Entry paths ... in two ways" enumeration (it omits the §6.2 non-retryable-during-resuming entry that a rebuild setup failure takes). FACT: Credential trace running → idle-suspended → released → message-resumed closes in the spec. The release returns the leases (SPEC-1g; earlier rounds refuted the claim that this was unstated). The driver runs the pre-claim availability check, assigns new leases after the `resuming` write and before `Resume` (SPEC-1g, SPEC-3q at §29.6 step 8), and §4.9 binds each lease to the SPIFFE URI of the pod that receives `AssignCredentials`. That pod is the replacement pod, so the restored runtime holds valid leases. §4.7.1 admission lists `AssignCredentials` as a bind-attempt RPC with a non-empty token, which is consistent with "under one token" — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:708, spec/04_system-components.md:1624
WATCHOUT: Under SPEC-1b, a restore older than `claimOrphanTimeout` (default 5 min) and a stopped attempt's claim are both reclaimed by the WPC orphan GC. Both increment `lenny_orphaned_claims_total` and feed the `SandboxClaimOrphanRateHigh` alert, whose spec text gives the cause as "gateways creating claims but crashing before persisting sessions". The troubleshooting row only says "Check gateway stability". This is docs-loop material at most, because the alert row's "potential gateway instability" is a gloss and does not become false — EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:526, docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md:355
UNVERIFIED: whether "idle timeout" in the §27.6 playground session-record revocation list means the agent-session idle bound, which now suspends rather than ends the session, or a playground-record idle timeout. The `pg:sess` key is the opaque browser-session id, so it is probably the latter. The docs loop should check this before editing web-playground.md — EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:207, spec/27_web-playground.md:243

FACT: I traced credential leases from running through idle suspension, release, and a resume triggered by a message. The idle release has a hold of zero and takes a checkpoint first (§6.2 release step 1 is unchanged). The release returns the session's leases (SPEC-1g; §7.1 step 23). The driver runs the §4.9 pre-claim check before it claims. It writes resume_pending → resuming (G+1), then AssignCredentials, then Resume, which binds the new lease's SPIFFE URI to the replacement pod (§4.9 SPIFFE-binding item 1). The commit to running then delivers the held messages (path 6). No staged statement contradicts another along this path. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:864, :1324, :1624; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:26

### [spec.5.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The staged spec edits add no CRD writer, finalizer, admission-webhook rule, or controller call on a request path. The resume driver claims pods through the existing gateway-owned `SandboxClaim` CREATE/status path, and the suspension release takes the existing session-end disposition (the gateway stamps the drain request and the WarmPoolController writes `Sandbox.status`). Leases, suspension reason, and held messages live in Postgres and Redis rather than etcd. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:619 (SandboxClaim row), :632 (gateway RBAC), :418 (WPC-written drain transitions).
FACT: SPEC-1g's order (setup, then credentials, then start) matches §7.1 steps 13-14, and its `AssignCredentials` (new lease) → `Resume` order matches the §4.7.8 Standard row. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md §7.1 step 13; spec/04_system-components.md:864.
USEFUL [review-log line 497, concurrent-pool orphan-GC FACT]: `Binder.Resume` always claims a fresh idle pod, so SPEC-1b's statement that no `pod_assignment` names the pod also holds on slot pools. I did not refile the slot-pod GC finding.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5b reverses the order in current §6.2 step 2 ("releases the pod ..., clears the session's pod binding") to clearing first and then releasing. On a single-session pod whose claim is older than `claimOrphanTimeout`, a WPC GC tick that lands between the clear and the claim transition drains a pod that would have recycled. Normal session end already has the same window once the terminal write lands, so I did not file it. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:229; spec/04_system-components.md:517.

### [spec.5.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: Under SPEC-1g the restore's Token Service call is `AssignCredentials`, which runs after the `resume_pending → resuming` write. A Token Service outage is therefore a post-claim failure that spends a retry under SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.**, and it is not a pre-claim failure. The pre-claim availability check reads pool utilization, cooldown, and health, and it does not call the Token Service. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1326 (pre-claim check), :1554 ("the replacement pod also requires `AssignCredentials` (which calls the Token Service)"); spec-changes.md:155; non-spec-changes.md:48 (hook precedes `AssignCredentials`).
WATCHOUT: The spec-changes edge case **Pre-claim failure.** (spec-changes.md:17) still lists "a Token Service outage", which was true before option A and is false after it. This round reports it as a finding. Remedy: delete the phrase.
FACT: The credential lease path traces cleanly for running → idle-suspended → released → resumed. The release returns the leases (SPEC-1g citing §7.1 step 23). The driver runs the pre-claim check before the claim. `AssignCredentials` runs after the entry write and before `Resume`, and it records the new pod's SPIFFE URI (spec/04_system-components.md:1624). The credential file is written before `session_start` (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:26). §4.7.1 admits `AssignCredentials` then `Resume` under one token (rule 4 creates the entry and `Resume` resolves it). No other finding.
UNVERIFIED: The 300 s `resuming` watchdog (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241, "matching the setup-command total timeout") also bounds a snapshotless rebuild, which runs materialization plus setup inside `resuming`. `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds` is per runtime and can be set above 300 s (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:204). A long setup may therefore never rebuild. This was not filed because resume-time setup predates 0092 (§15.1 SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED "resume-time setup"). A non-spec or human reviewer should decide whether it matters.

### [spec.5.review-performance.1]
FACT: The spec's capacity budgets that 0092 touches are estimates keyed on session arrival: Tier 3 claim rate ~30/s is Little's Law over `capacityPlanning.avgSessionDurationSeconds` 333 s (spec/16_observability.md:609,616), and the etcd and Postgres tables (spec/04_system-components.md:469-475, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:64-72) carry no per-resume term. With a 333 s mean session, the 900 s idle suspension touches only the long tail, and `base_demand_p95` is observed from actual claims, so resume-driven claims feed the warm-pool formula on their own. Nothing to file. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:555-559
FACT: The per-delivery `StampSync` admission write adds no new amplification class, because §6.2 already writes `last_agent_activity_at` to Postgres on each client message and each agent_output/tool_use event. Only proxy-mode SSE chunks are coalesced to 1/s. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:265-270
FACT: The Redis failure-behavior table lists no DLQ or inbox row (pre-existing). The §7.2 durable-mode text already makes the DLQ a Redis-only store. Extending DLQ custody to `suspended` sessions does not create a new loss class without a concrete loss case; see the Traps entry "Refuted: store-outage degradation". — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:212-226, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:308-312
WATCHOUT: A Token Service outage now blocks every message-driven resume of an idle-suspended session: the pre-claim check fails, the session goes to `resume_pending`, then `awaiting_client_action`, then expires. Before 0092, an idle running session kept its leases. This follows from the owner's option A and the lease-releasing suspension, which was rejected as an alternative, so it is not a finding. The runbook sentence "sessions holding active leases are unaffected" stays literally true. — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:826
UNVERIFIED: In the §10.1.1 Postgres lease fallback (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, transaction-scoped), SPEC-2a's lease retention in every non-terminal state could pin one open transaction and row lock per podless session during a Redis outage. Off-holder store writes (podless `/resume`) would then block on that row lock. The spec does not say whether the fallback transaction stays open for the life of the lease, so I did not file this. The owner of the §10.1.1 fallback should check. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:29
USEFUL [Traps: Refuted: the 7-day suspended population]: this entry saved re-deriving the lease-renewal and Redis-ops math for the 7-day podless population.

### [spec.5.review-reliability.1]
FACT: Credential lease trace for running -> idle-suspended -> released -> message-resumed holds in the staged spec. The suspension release returns the leases (SPEC-1g general sentence). The driver runs the pre-claim check before the claim, and assigns new leases after the `resuming` write and before `Resume`. Proxy-mode SPIFFE binding is recorded at `AssignCredentials` time, so the new lease binds to the replacement pod. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1624 ("At `AssignCredentials` time, the gateway records the issuing pod's SPIFFE URI"); spec/04_system-components.md:864 (Standard replacement-pod order `AssignCredentials` (new lease) -> `Resume`); spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:37-39 (finalize assigns leases after setup, before start).
FACT: Checkpoint selection on resume keys on MAX(coordination_generation) over existing manifest rows. The resume driver's entry increment does not break selection, because generation only rises and selection takes the max over rows that exist. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:171.
FACT: REG-COORDLEASE is defined with its Postgres fallback in §28, so "the replica holding REG-COORDLEASE" in SPEC-1b covers a Redis outage at spec level. The non-spec "lease read error = not holder" rule is a code question. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:248, :353.
WATCHOUT: A leased credential minted by an attempt whose replica crashed before its commit or release has no named reclaimer except lease TTL expiry (default 3600 s) and the session-wide terminal release. Judged bounded and below the bar; do not file it as a leak unless a lease that never expires is shown. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1253-1263; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:230.
WATCHOUT: The 300 s `resuming` watchdog equals the default setup cap (`setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds`, which may be raised per runtime or left unbounded), so a snapshotless rebuild with long setup cannot finish. Not filed: SPEC-1b already accepts that a restore older than `claimOrphanTimeout` (5 min) loses its pod, and OD-1 left the watchdog bullet unchanged. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:202; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:204.
UNVERIFIED: SPEC-1g says "one lease per provider the resolution names". §4.9 says that at finalize, a provider with no available credential is skipped. If "resolution" includes a provider that is exhausted at resume, the attempt fails and spends a retry where finalize would have skipped that provider. The non-spec reuses `assignCredentials`, so the behavior probably matches finalize. A non-spec reviewer should confirm whether `assignCredentials` skips or fails. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:155; spec/04_system-components.md:1434.

### [spec.8.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: A podless `suspended` session keeps no renewed REG-COORDLEASE and is not Sweeper-adopted; a replica serving a §29.3 coordinator row for it adopts on demand through the §10.1.2 handoff (SPEC-2a) — BECAUSE the renewed population otherwise grows with creation rate x 604800 s (about 1.2M leases and 40k renewals/s at Tier 3 with 1% abandonment), beyond the §12.4 ~1,000/s row, the §17.8.2 ~10K ops/s Sentinel, and the Postgres advisory-lock fallback — ALTERNATIVES: restating §12.4/§17.8.2 budgets (no default lifetime fits without guessing the abandoned fraction); per-row §29.3 any-replica carve-outs (events JSON still needs a holder); longer TTL (per-state lease semantics, same scaling).
DECISION: The backlog resume of a podless row runs on any replica (SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** lead; CODE-6 item 2.2 gate only when the row names a pod) — BECAUSE its action is the `suspended → resume_pending` store write any replica may make, and with no renewed lease a lease-gated podless backlog resume would strand a held message until the 7-day expiry — ALTERNATIVES: keeping SPEC-5i2 item 2 (deleted; it made the podless backlog resume lease-gated in §10.1.5).
WATCHOUT: Do not re-add podless `suspended` to SPEC-2a's renewed or adopted lists, and do not stage §12.4/§17.8.2 capacity edits; with podless `suspended` out of renewal those rows stay true. The summary's rejected-alternatives entry "Renewing the lease in podless `suspended`" is the one home of the reason. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md "Lease renewals (`SET` with TTL) | ~1,000/s"; spec/17_deployment-topology.md "Coordination (session leases) | Sentinel ... | ~10K"
WATCHOUT: Do not add an explicit lease Release at the zero-hold or interrupt-hold pod release; stopping renewal lets the lease lapse at TTL. CODE-5 item 1 now drops the shipped `priorHolder == s.replicaID` renew for a podless `suspended` row, which is what makes it lapse. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go `eligible := bound || priorHolder == s.replicaID || adoptable`
FACT: No off-holder forward exists (LNK-INTERREPLICA is a spec link with no implementation), and terminate runs locally through `handleTransition`, so the on-demand adoption clause has no code caller yet; RECORDS-1 item 7(l) files it and item 8's WIRED row is scoped to the Sweeper adoption. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/route_table.go `handleTransition(session.EndpointTerminate`; spec/28_communication-channels.md `LNK-INTERREPLICA` row
WATCHOUT: The summary's "Releasing the lease with the binding" rejected alternative was left as is: it concerns an explicit release in every bound-less state, which the design still rejects. A later round may read it as contradicting the podless lapse; it does not, because nothing releases the lease and renewal simply stops in podless `suspended` only.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: no step is falsified (SPEC-5i2 keeps its id; S10 and S16 name deliverables only). Nothing to close.
OPEN: The summary's known-gaps list (one bullet per RECORDS-1 filing) has no bullet for the new RECORDS-1 item 7(l); the list does not claim completeness, so it was not edited. The reconciliation pass may add one.

DECISION: Podless `suspended` keeps no renewed REG-COORDLEASE and is not Sweeper-adopted; a replica serving a §29.3 row that needs the coordinator adopts it on demand through the §10.1.2 handoff; the podless backlog resume runs on any replica (it is a `suspended → resume_pending` store write any replica may write) — BECAUSE the finding now cites spec budgets (§12.4 "only row that scales directly with concurrent session count", §17.8.2 ~10K coordination Sentinel, §12.4 Postgres advisory-lock fallback), and a podless suspended population grows with creation rate x 604800 s; `awaiting_client_action` stays renewed because §7.3 bounds it at maxAwaitingClientActionSeconds (900 s) — ALTERNATIVES: restating §12.4/§17.8.2 over the 7-day population (no default lifetime bounds it without a guess at the abandoned fraction, and the Postgres fallback stays infeasible); per-state lease TTL (hair, still scales); making podless terminate/delete any-replica like `created` (needs a §29.3 row edit per route and leaves the events-JSON and resolution rows unanswered).

FACT: a pod with no fenced generation for a session rejects none of that session's RPCs on generation grounds; a restored pod is never fenced (pre-existing, and SPEC-2b's podless step-2 skip does not widen it because the replacement pod is named nowhere until the resuming → running commit) — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:41

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the suspension release moves the session inbox to the DLQ after the guarded `PodAssignment` clear and before the pod release (SPEC-5b step 2 cites the §7.2 inbox-to-DLQ migration; CODE-6 item 4 step 3 calls `migrateInboxOnResumePending`) — BECAUSE a podless suspended session holds no lease, so the old holder's in-memory inbox was otherwise stranded and escaped the terminal DLQ drain — ALTERNATIVES: keep the lease while the inbox is non-empty (reintroduces podless lease-holding), restore a SPEC-5c drain on the podless `resume_pending` write (drains only the writer's own empty inbox), accept the loss (breaks the §7.2 alive-coordinator guarantee).
WATCHOUT: the migration must follow the clear. Before the clear, a held-pod resume's `deliverBuffered` can be mid-`Send` on an inbox entry the drain moves to the DLQ, duplicating delivery; after the clear the step-3 guard and the `resumeHeldPod` guard exclude it, so no delivery lock is needed. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 step 3 and tail sentence "The release takes no delivery lock."
FACT: migrated entries are scored at acceptance time (SPEC-5z item 7), so they predate `SuspendedAt` and the interrupt **Backlog resume.** check (DLQ entries accepted after `SuspendedAt`) does not count them; an `Idle` release counts them in the DLQ. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 2 **`HeldLen`.**
FACT: `migrateInboxOnResumePending` is the only wrapper of `MigrateInboxToDLQ` in the session server and its only production caller was failure.go:360; its doc comment still says "EXPIRE" for durable mode, which CODE-4 item 18 removes. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messaging.go:102-118

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: A failed admission write (any StampSync error other than ErrSessionSuspended) fails open: the message is delivered unrecorded. SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** states it in one sentence citing §12.3; CODE-6 item 5.5 puts the policy in StampSync (log, no lastWrite, return nil) — BECAUSE §12.3 **Behavior during Postgres failover:** and §10.1.3 say existing sessions continue, and the shipped async Stamp drops store failures — ALTERNATIVES: fail closed with staged §12.3/§10.1.3 exceptions (stops delivery to every running session for up to 30 s); hold in the DLQ (client gets `queued`, delivered up to 15 min later); classify the error in PodExecutor.Send item 5.2 (puts suspension policy in the executor, changes 5.2 and its test).
WATCHOUT: Fail-open leaves a residual window: while the store is unreachable no `running → suspended` write can land either, but a SuspendIdle that read a stale replica row and lands its write just as the primary returns could suspend over an unrecorded delivery. Do not add a per-replica "unrecorded delivery" marker to close it; the check and write cannot be atomic without the store. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**; pgstore Get reads the replica while Update goes to the primary.
WATCHOUT: StampSync has no timeout of its own; a hung primary stalls Send until the caller's context ends. A timeout knob was deliberately not added in this round; file it separately if needed. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 5.1
MISTAKE: Standing context **Refuted: store-outage degradation.** told reviewers to refute the admission write as a Postgres dependency; that half was wrong (confirmed in spec.1 G2) and was cut from the entry. The old CODE-6 item 5.5 justification ("a delivery already depends on the store") was also false for a running session's message path.
FACT: The shipped Stamper.Stamp runs `go s.persist(...)` off the request path and drops failures. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go (Stamp, persist)

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The release migrates the in-memory inbox to the DLQ right after the guarded clear of `PodAssignment` and before the pod release (SPEC-5b step 2 by citing the §7.2 inbox-to-DLQ migration paragraph; CODE-6 item 4 step 3 calls `migrateInboxOnResumePending`). It takes no delivery lock. BECAUSE the clear's guard and the `resumeHeldPod` guard already exclude a held-pod resume after the clear, so no `deliverBuffered` on this replica can be reading the inbox (the next one needs a driver claim and restore). The migrated entries keep their acceptance scores (SPEC-5z item 7), so they are older than `SuspendedAt`, the interrupt backlog check does not count them, and the next resume or the terminal DLQ drain handles them. ALTERNATIVES: migrate before the clear. Rejected: a held-pod resume during the checkpoint runs `deliverBuffered` mid-`Send` while the migration drains, which delivers the entry and also leaves it in the DLQ, so it is delivered twice. Closing that would need the delivery lock in the release, and then the Design delivery-lock paragraph, CODE-6 item 4 ("The release takes no delivery lock"), and summary watch-out "covers only the message paths" would all change. Also rejected: keep the lease while the inbox is non-empty, which brings back the podless lease-holding that redesign 5 removed. Also rejected: accept the loss in SPEC-5c, which breaks the §7.2 default-mode guarantee while the replica is alive.
WATCHOUT: This is a migration at the pod release. It is NOT the "inbox-to-DLQ migration on entry to `suspended`" that Standing context Traps forbids. That trap concerns entry. While the pod is held, the coordinator keeps the inbox and `deliverBuffered` reads it. Do not move the migration to the `running → suspended` write. EVIDENCE: review-log.md Standing context "Do not restore".
WATCHOUT: Some windows of a few milliseconds, between the clear and the end of the migration, remain unhandled. In one, a terminate served by another replica runs its terminal drain before the migrated entries land in the DLQ, so those senders get no `target_terminated`. In the other, a crash loses the inbox, which is §7.2's accepted default-mode crash loss. The release adds no re-read for the first case. Refile it only with a path that widens the window.
FACT: `MigrateInboxToDLQ` drains first and then enqueues each entry with `DefaultDLQTTL`, which scores it at `EnqueuedAt` + TTL. `migrateInboxOnResumePending` logs an error and never returns one, so the release continues after a failed migration, as §7.2's failure sentence states for the transition. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/coordinator.go:104-134; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messaging.go:112-118.
CORRECTS [review-log-archive OPEN at :542 "inbox message present when a session enters suspended ... never migrated"]: the release migration closes it for the default inbox. Durable-mode entries stay in Redis, where any replica's terminal drain or driver can reach them.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: an admission write that fails for any reason other than finding the row `suspended` lets the delivery proceed unrecorded; the policy lives in `StampSync` (logs, returns nil, records no `lastWrite`), so the executor contract "an admitter error refuses the Send" (CODE-6 item 5.2) is unchanged — BECAUSE §12.3 **Behavior during Postgres failover:** and §10.1.3 item 1 continue existing sessions, and the `running → suspended` guarded write needs the same primary — ALTERNATIVES: fail closed with staged exceptions in §12.3 and §10.1.3 (stops delivery to every running session for up to 30 s); hold the message in the DLQ on a store error (a running session's held entry waits for the next path-2 message or the idle suspension, up to 15 min); classify the error in `Send` against the sentinel (puts suspension policy in the executor).
FACT: the session row read on the message paths goes to the read replica (`pgstore.Get` uses `s.read`), while `Update` goes to the primary, so CODE-6 item 5.5's old reason "a delivery already depends on the store" was false during a primary failover. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:354-372, :404
WATCHOUT: residual window of the fail-open: a suspension committed just before the primary fails, read stale from the replica, can admit an unrecorded delivery to a `suspended` row whose pod the next `sweepSuspended` tick releases. Judged below the bar against a 30 s delivery outage; refile only with a reproduction that does not need a stale replica read during a failover.
WATCHOUT: `StampSync` runs on the caller's context; during a failover each `Send` waits for the pool's connect/acquire error before proceeding. The shipped async `persist` bounds itself at 5 s. A later round may want the same bound on `StampSync`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:222-245
CORRECTS [Standing context "Refuted: store-outage degradation"]: the admission write's Postgres dependency was a real availability conflict with §12.3/§10.1.3 (confirmed finding, spec loop redesign-5 round 1); only the Redis-outage DLQ-peek half of that entry still stands.

### [spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1]

DECISION: filed one finding: the podless-`suspended` lease drop (SPEC-2a) strands the in-memory (`durableInbox: false`) inbox of an interrupt-suspended session on the replica that held it, and SPEC-5c deletes the only spec sentence that drained that inbox to the DLQ on the podless resume. BECAUSE: the inbox-to-DLQ migration runs on the replica that writes `resume_pending`, on its own inbox (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messaging.go:112-118, failure.go:360), while SPEC-5i lets any replica write `suspended → resume_pending` and SPEC-2a lets any replica adopt; Backlog resume counts the inbox only for `Idle`, so an interrupt suspension is released with inbox entries in place. ALTERNATIVES: filing it against the §7.2 Durability row alone (rejected: the loss is a new cause, not a crash, and the fix sits at the release in SPEC-5b).
FACT: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:232 (the "Once the pod is released" paragraph SPEC-5c replaces) is the only spec text that drains the in-memory inbox on the podless `suspended → resume_pending` edge. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:232.
WATCHOUT: the Traps entry "Do not restore ... an inbox-to-DLQ migration on entry to `suspended`" predates redesign 5, when the coordinator kept the lease through podless `suspended`; a migration at the release (after the hold, before the binding clear) is a different site and does not reintroduce the redesign-3 race the trap guards. DECISION: filed one finding: SPEC-1g says "a credential that is unavailable at that check is a claim failure under item 1", but §4.9 **Pre-Claim Credential Availability Check** rejects only when no provider in the intersection has an available credential and skips the others (spec/04_system-components.md:1326, :1434). The code agrees with §4.9: `credrouter.PreClaim` errors only when `!res.Available()` (pkg/gateway/llmproxy/credrouter/preclaim.go:104-110), and the resolution it returns names only the providers that resolved, which is what `assignCredentials` leases. ALTERNATIVES: leave it as wording (rejected: it is the only statement of the restore's credential gate, and it disagrees with the section it cites).
FACT: `assignCredentials` mints one lease per provider in `req.CredentialPools`, and that map is `PreClaimResult.PoolAssignments`, so "the resolution" in SPEC-1g means the providers that resolved at the check. ### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: SPEC-2a drops REG-COORDLEASE when a `suspended` row stops naming a pod, which strands the default-mode (`durableInbox: false`) in-memory inbox of an interrupt-suspended session on the old holder. Inbox entries buffered under §7.2 path 5 (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:332) survive an interrupt, SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** counts the inbox only for `Idle`, and so no `resume_pending` write happens on the holder at the release. A later resume is written by any replica (its inbox drain finds nothing) and adopted by any replica, and a podless terminate or lifetime expiry runs on the serving replica, so the §7.2 inbox delivery and the inbox-drain-on-terminal (spec/07:352) cannot reach those entries. BECAUSE the previous design kept the lease (and the inbox) on the holder through podless suspension (archive DECISION at review-log-archive.md:236 relied on that). Suggested remedy: the holder moves its in-memory inbox into the DLQ at the release (SPEC-5b step 2), scored by acceptance time per SPEC-5z item 7, which leaves the interrupt backlog predicate untouched. ALTERNATIVES: counting the inbox in the release-time backlog check for every reason (rejected: resumes an interrupt suspension at once, the Standing-context trap); keeping the lease (rejected by the redesign-5 directive).
FACT: The DLQ-TTL scoring objection that rejected an inbox-to-DLQ move at pod release (review-log-archive.md:213, :236) is moot after SPEC-5z item 7 (acceptance-time score) and the DLQ TTL being enforced only in recovering states. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5z item 7, SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** last sentence.
FACT: The Standing-context trap "Do not restore an inbox-to-DLQ migration on entry to `suspended`" concerns entry, not the release; a move at release does not trigger the interrupt backlog predicate because the moved entries were accepted before `SuspendedAt`.
WATCHOUT: OD-P4 (events JSON vs §15.1 "retained replay buffer", spec/15_external-api-surface.md:708 and spec/10_gateway-internals.md:406) is a recorded open decision; do not file it. EVIDENCE: summary.md OD-P4.
FACT: §15.1 terminate precondition admits neither `input_required` nor `resuming`, so the SPEC-2d item 2 state cell ("the states this route accepts in which the session holds REG-COORDLEASE") agrees with SPEC-2a. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:649.

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]

DECISION: filed one finding. With `durableInbox: false`, the in-memory inbox entries of an interrupt-suspended session are stranded once SPEC-2a drops the lease for a podless row. BECAUSE: path-5 entries buffered before the interrupt stay in the releasing replica's MemoryInbox, the interrupt backlog predicate excludes the inbox, and after the lease lapses any adopter's **Delivery of held messages.** reads only its own inbox. The enterResumePending migration runs on whichever replica writes `resume_pending`. ALTERNATIVES: (1) keep the lease while the inbox is non-empty. Rejected because it brings back a per-row probe. (2) Migrate inbox to DLQ on entry to `suspended`. Rejected because Standing context bars it, since it would let pre-suspension entries undo the interrupt. The suggested remedy is a migration at the SPEC-5b step 2 clear, scored by acceptance time. Those entries predate SuspendedAt, so they do not trigger a resume.
FACT: interrupt does not touch the inbox. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go (no inbox reference). The default-mode guarantee is "does not drop undelivered messages while the coordinating replica remains alive". EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:312.
FACT: §6.2 says "The orphan session reconciler skips sessions in `suspended` state with no pod binding." EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:236. It is consistent with the podless no-lease rule.
FACT: Every §29.3 route a podless `suspended` session admits has a server after SPEC-2d, SPEC-3j, and SPEC-5l. sweepSuspended does a per-replica listing with an in-memory lifetime compare and no Redis call for podless rows (non-spec CODE-6 item 2.2). The no-per-row-probe constraint holds.
WATCHOUT: the §10.1.5 **Session with no bound pod.** sentence "holds no stream, pending tool call, or buffered event" is false for a replica that holds MemoryInbox entries. If the inbox finding is fixed by migration at release, this sentence becomes true again, so do not edit it separately. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:143.

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the DLQ TTL runs from the later of acceptance and the session's entry to the recovering state it is in (SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** last sentence, its one home); scores stay acceptance + TTL (SPEC-5z item 7) for order; code gate is `sweepDLQExpiry` skipping a row whose `UpdatedAt` is under `DefaultDLQTTL` old (CODE-6 item 12) — BECAUSE entries migrated at the suspension release were accepted before `SuspendedAt`, the interrupt hold (900 s) is at least the TTL (900 s), so an acceptance clock expired every migrated entry at the first `resume_pending` sweep — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "most recent entry to `resume_pending`" clock (needs a new column, expires during a slow `resuming`, wrong on the held-pod `suspended → awaiting_client_action` edge); migration-time scores (break acceptance order read by `deliverBuffered` and `CountAcceptedAfter`); deleting the release-time migration (strands the in-memory inbox of a lease-less podless session).
CORRECTS [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]: the FACT that the DLQ-TTL objection to an inbox-to-DLQ move at pod release is "moot after SPEC-5z item 7" is wrong: an acceptance-based clock has already run out when recovery starts. The clock now starts no earlier than entry to the recovering state.
CORRECTS [Standing context Settled **DLQ TTL home.**]: "TTL counted from acceptance" is replaced by "from the later of acceptance and entry to the current recovering state"; the enforcement states and the §15.4 pointer are unchanged.
WATCHOUT: `UpdatedAt` is a proxy for state entry; any later write (for example `AdvanceLastSeq`) pushes expiry later. Accepted: retention only grows, and the terminal drain removes what remains. Do not add a state-entry column to tighten it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go `sweepDLQExpiry`; non-spec-changes.md **Watchdog anchor.** edge case.
OPEN: the §7.2 durable-inbox Per-message TTL row (trimmer, `enqueued_at` clock) has the same acceptance-clock defect for interrupt-held durable entries. The trimmer is unbuilt (RECORDS-1 7(j)); this round did not stage it (design ruled it a separate finding). — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md Per-message TTL row.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The DLQ TTL runs from the later of acceptance and the session's entry to the recovering state it is in (SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** last sentence is the one home); the score stays acceptance + TTL for order, and `sweepDLQExpiry` gates each row on `now - row.UpdatedAt >= DefaultDLQTTL` before calling `SweepExpired` — BECAUSE expiry = max(score, entry + TTL) exactly under that gate, with no new column, and `sweepResumePending`, `sweepResuming`, and `ActiveAge` already use `UpdatedAt` as the state-entry time — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "most recent entry to `resume_pending`" (expires pre-recovery entries mid-restore in `resuming`, and gives no window on the direct held-pod `suspended → awaiting_client_action` edge, and needs a new timestamp column); revert SPEC-5z item 7 to migration-time scores (breaks acceptance order that `deliverBuffered` and `CountAcceptedAfter` read); drop the release-time migration (strands the in-memory inbox, see spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
CORRECTS [spec.1.review-mechanism.1 FACT "DLQ-TTL scoring objection ... is moot"]: false under an acceptance clock, because an interrupt-held entry is at least `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` (900 s) old at the release and the TTL default is 900 s; the objection is moot only under the entry-to-state clock this round stages.
CORRECTS [Standing context Settled "DLQ TTL home"]: the TTL is no longer "counted from acceptance"; it runs from the later of acceptance and entry to the current recovering state. The home sentence is unchanged.
WATCHOUT: `UpdatedAt` advances on any row write, so a non-state write in a recovering state (for example `AdvanceLastSeq` in `resuming`) extends retention. This is the same approximation the resume-window sweep accepts; do not add a state-entry column for it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go sweepResumePending (`now.Sub(row.UpdatedAt)`)

A later round should decide whether SPEC-5z item 4 points the trimmer's clock at **Delivery of held messages.**.

### [spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: no mechanism-lens findings in the round-2 spec staging — BECAUSE the two round-1 fixes (SPEC-5b step 2 release-time inbox migration, SPEC-5f admission-failure delivery) trace cleanly against SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**, SPEC-2a, and the §7.2 migration paragraph, and every §29.3 row admitting `suspended` has a serving-replica outcome for a podless session — ALTERNATIVES: filing the two leads below; both fell under the bar.

The Idle release check still counts those entries through its inbox term. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:314
WATCHOUT: Under the default 900 s hold and 900 s `maxResumeWindowSeconds`, an interrupt-suspended session's pre-suspension inbox entries migrated at release have always passed their acceptance-scored TTL by the time the session resumes, so they expire as `dlq_ttl_expired` at the first sweep in `resume_pending`. SPEC-5i states this ("an entry whose TTL elapsed while ... `suspended` expires at the first expiry sweep"). The durable trimmer (TTL also counted from acceptance) behaves the same way, so do not file it as a regression without a contradicting spec sentence. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:525, :723
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5f's new admission-failure branch (deliver unrecorded) leaves a check-then-write window: the idle sweeper reads no turn in flight, then a `Send` whose admission write fails starts a turn, then the sweeper's guarded write lands. For that to happen, the admission write has to fail and the suspend write has to succeed within one round-trip, at the moment the idle clock fires. I judged it below the bar. Refile only with a mechanism that makes the window wider than one round-trip. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:480
USEFUL [spec.1.fix-G1.1]: its WATCHOUT on ordering (migration after the clear) and its FACT on acceptance-time scoring settled the release-path trace without re-deriving it.

### [spec.2.review-performance.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings in spec round 2 (redesign 5). BECAUSE the round-1-to-2 spec diff makes two changes, and neither adds a write class or a store dependency that reaches the top tier. The SPEC-5b step 2 inbox-to-DLQ migration runs once per pod release, as one Redis pipeline on the releasing (coordinating) replica. The SPEC-5f admission-write fail-open removes the Postgres dependency of delivery that round 1 confirmed. Re-traced: Postgres write IOPS (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:64-70, Tier 3 ~1,300/s, with session state updates at ~300/s), and §6.2 already writes `last_agent_activity_at` on each qualifying event. No etcd write is added. Lease adoption covers only `resume_pending` rows reached from podless `suspended`, so it is bounded by the resume rate. ALTERNATIVES: refiling the fail-open residual window. A transient `StampSync` error that coincides with a landed `SuspendIdle` write, with no stale replica read needed, admits an unrecorded turn that the zero-hold release then cuts, because `ReleaseSuspendedPod` does not check `TurnInFlight`. I rejected it as below the bar: it needs two primary-side events to coincide, and the redesign-5 r1 WATCHOUT already weighed the window. A later round could close it with a spec remedy: the release clears nothing while a turn is in flight, since the releaser, `SuspendIdle`, and `Send` all run on the coordinating replica.
FACT: The coordination Sweeper calls `ListFilter{}` for every tenant and makes a Redis `leases.Get` call for every non-terminal row on every replica each sweep, so podless `suspended` rows pay one GET per replica per sweep even though they hold no lease. This cost predates 0092 and is recorded as RECORDS-1 7(l). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:296-327
FACT: The §7.2 inbox-to-DLQ migration that SPEC-5b step 2 now cites is scoped `durableInbox: false only`. In durable mode the release moves nothing and the inbox stays in Redis. On a Redis write failure the in-memory entries are lost and `lenny_inbox_drain_failure_total` counts the loss, which is the same exposure as the shipped migration at `resume_pending`. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:314-320
USEFUL [Standing context "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population", "Refuted: store-outage degradation"; ledger 930-947 fail-open DECISION/WATCHOUTs]: these covered the Sweeper GET, `HeldLen`, Redis-reset DLQ loss, and fail-open residual-window candidates.

### [spec.2.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed one finding. The round-1 release migration (SPEC-5b step 2) moves an interrupt-suspended session's pre-suspension inbox entries into the DLQ, scored at acceptance + maxResumeWindowSeconds (SPEC-5z item 7). The TTL is measured from acceptance and enforced in every recovering state (SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** last sentence), and the hold (900 s) is at least the TTL (900 s). So every migrated entry is already past its TTL at the release, and the 5 s `sweepDLQExpiry` tick in `resume_pending`/`resuming` expires it before the driver's commit delivers it. With the pod still held, the same entries are delivered. BECAUSE: the fix exists to carry these entries to the next resume, and it does not. ALTERNATIVES: score at release time (rejected: it only moves the clock, and a 7-day podless suspension still outlives the TTL); exclude migrated entries from expiry (rejected: it adds a second class of entry).
CORRECTS [spec.1.review-mechanism.1 FACT "The DLQ-TTL scoring objection ... is moot after SPEC-5z item 7 ... and the DLQ TTL being enforced only in recovering states"]: it is not moot. An acceptance-time score makes an entry older than the TTL expire at the first sweep after the session enters `resume_pending`. Recovering-state enforcement only defers the expiry to that moment. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:525 last sentence; pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166 (5 s tick), :684-705.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1 DECISION "the next resume or the terminal DLQ drain handles them"]: the next resume expires them with `dlq_ttl_expired` and does not deliver them.
FACT: the durable-mode trimmer has the same acceptance-based clock: `per_message_ttl` from `enqueued_at`, run in recovering states. So durable-mode pre-suspension inbox entries of a released interrupt session also expire on resume. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:301, :303.
UNVERIFIED (non-spec loop): the pod release's credential release is session-wide (`Binder.releaseCredentials` → `ReleaseSession`, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/credentiallease.go:139-144). `slotAccountLocks` orders it against a same-replica resume only. If the release outlives the REG-COORDLEASE TTL after the clear, a peer could adopt the session through a later `resume_pending` write and assign leases that the old release then revokes. SPEC-1g ("returns its leases"; "never revokes a lease that another attempt holds") states this correctly, so the issue is in the code only.

### [spec.3.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed one finding. The durable-inbox trimmer still counts per_message_ttl from `enqueued_at`, and SPEC-5z item 4 widens its activation to `resuming`. As a result, in durable mode the pre-suspension inbox entries of an interrupt-suspended session (always >= the 900 s hold old at release) expire within one 30 s trimmer tick of `resume_pending`, before the restore commit delivers them. Default mode carries the same entries through the release migration under SPEC-5i's state-entry DLQ clock. BECAUSE: the spec.2 fix gave the DLQ a state-entry clock and left the durable half as an OPEN ("a later round should decide"). Spec text, spec/07:301 "recovery-window cleanup bound ... while the session is in a recovering state", already supports the state-entry reading. ALTERNATIVES: not filing it as pre-existing. Rejected because item 4 rewrites that row and SPEC-5i now delivers the same message class in one mode and expires it in the other.
FACT: the lease rule is stated once (SPEC-2a). The serving replica, a store write, or the relay serves every §29.3 row a podless `suspended` session admits (SPEC-2d, SPEC-3j, SPEC-5l). The podless lifetime expiry runs from the per-replica watchdog listing with no Redis probe. No staged spec text runs a periodic per-row probe over podless rows (OD-P3 holds the alternative). EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:166, :200-202, :524.
FACT: the credential trace holds in spec. The suspension release returns the old pod's leases (SPEC-1g citing §7.1 step 23 at spec/07:54). The restore runs the pre-claim check before the claim and assigns leases after the `resuming` write and before Resume. A stopped attempt releases only its own leases. A replica crash mid-attempt leaks leases until their TTL, which is the same as the finalize path, and no lease reaper exists anywhere in spec/04 §4.9, so this predates 0092.
WATCHOUT: residuals that are stranded until the next resume (a failed hold-time `resume_pending` write, or a failed or skipped release-time check) are OD-P3 territory and stated in SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**. Do not file them as reliability findings. EVIDENCE: summary.md:111, spec-changes.md:524.
WATCHOUT: a failed release-time inbox migration loses in-memory entries even though the release is voluntary (nothing forces it the way a pod failure forces `resume_pending`). spec.2.fix-G1.2 weighed aborting and rejected it. Retrying the migration while the releasing replica lives was not weighed. Judged below the bar because the loss is stated, counted, and alerted.

### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5z item 13 narrows the §7.2 default-mode Crash recovery row's trigger to adoption where the §10.1.2 step 0 read (state and pod_assignment, as SPEC-2b stages) finds `running`, `input_required`, or `suspended` with a pod; deletes the "(e.g., during a prior `resume_pending` transition)" example — BECAUSE under SPEC-2a a podless suspension lets the lease lapse and the later `resume_pending` adoption would emit a false `coordinator_failover` with `messagesPreservedInDLQ: 0` (defined as data loss); the inbox-to-DLQ migration empties the inbox at `resume_pending` and at the pod release (SPEC-5z item 11) — ALTERNATIVES: an exemption clause for a podless `suspended` row (unimplementable: CODE-5 `adoptedState` never adopts that row, so the adopter sees only `resume_pending`); a lapse-reason flag (new state for a notification); direct lease take on the podless `resume_pending` write (reopens settled SPEC-2a).
WATCHOUT: the reason for the narrowed trigger lives here, not in the row. Do not add an exemption sentence for podless `suspended` to the row; the trigger is positive. The row's "0 means lost" wording for a crash in `running` with an empty inbox is a separate pre-existing issue — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md (§7.2 default-mode Crash recovery row)
FACT: `inbox_cleared` appears in spec/ and docs/ only in the §7.2 default-mode Crash recovery row — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:293

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Close the podless-suspended `inbox_cleared` finding with SPEC-5z item 13, which replaces the §7.2 default-mode Crash recovery row's trigger "When a new gateway replica takes over coordination (via lease reacquisition)" with adoption of a session the adopter's step 0 read finds in `running`, `input_required`, or `suspended` with a pod named, and deletes "(e.g., during a prior `resume_pending` transition)". BECAUSE those are the only adopted states where the dead coordinator held an in-memory inbox: the inbox-to-DLQ migration empties it at the `resume_pending` write and at the pod release (SPEC-5z item 11), so `resume_pending` and `awaiting_client_action` adoption loses nothing. ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's exemption sentence ("a replica that acquires the lease of a `suspended` session whose row named no pod") cannot be implemented, because CODE-5's `adoptedState` never adopts a podless `suspended` row; the adopter reads `resume_pending` and cannot tell a podless-suspension lapse from a crash in `resume_pending`. A row flag recording why the lease lapsed would be a new column for a notification. Having the writer of the podless `resume_pending` take the lease itself changes SPEC-2a and still leaves a crash in `resume_pending` emitting a false "lost" event with a count of 0.
WATCHOUT: An `inbox_cleared` trigger keyed on how the lease lapsed cannot be implemented. Key it on the step 0 state the adopter reads. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 `adoptedState`; spec-changes.md SPEC-2b step 0 SELECT.

FACT: The §7.2 default-mode Crash recovery row (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md, "**Default mode (`durableInbox: false`) — in-memory inbox:**" table) is the only `inbox_cleared` site in spec/ and docs/. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:293

### [spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1]

Under the post-change spec, then, every resume of an idle-suspended session reports a coordinator failover, and when no message was held it reports an inbox loss. ### [spec.6.review-performance.1]
FACT: Every concurrent-session admission cap counts non-terminal rows: tenant quota `CountActiveSessions`, plus per-user, per-runtime, and global (`!session.IsTerminal`). The spec says the same thing in the §8.10 orphan note ("for as long as it remains non-terminal it counts toward ... per-user concurrent-session admission cap"). A podless `suspended` session therefore holds a cap slot for up to `gateway.maxSuspendedSessionSeconds` while holding no pod, lease, or credential. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:41-47; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/memstore/memstore.go:377-389; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:1111; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:8,23
FACT: Before this proposal, an idle `running` session ended at the idle bound, whose default was the effective maxSessionAgeSeconds (7200 s). After it, the session is idle-suspended at 900 s and stays non-terminal for 604800 s. A client that never terminates its sessions therefore holds a cap slot about 84 times longer. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:416; spec-changes.md SPEC-5f, SPEC-5g
FACT: §29.3 rows give a podless `suspended` session a server in every case. Messages are covered by SPEC-5l, terminate and delete by SPEC-2d item 2 (the delete row says "same as terminate"), interaction resolution by SPEC-2d item 3, events JSON by SPEC-2d item 4, and resume by SPEC-3j. The interrupt and upload rows are scoped to `running`. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:528-575
FACT: In the staged spec, only the coordinating replica of a held-pod row and the releasing replica run the backlog probe (SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**). The shipped Sweeper's per-row `leases.Get` over every non-terminal row on every replica (coordination.go:319) is code, and Traps "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population" covers it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:296-342
WATCHOUT: §12.5's 7-day artifact retention TTL equals the default suspended lifetime. I did not file it: the TTL anchor is unstated, and "keep latest 2 per active session" plus "delete all checkpoints when session terminates" suggest live sessions' checkpoints are not TTL-reaped. Refile only with a spec sentence that TTL-reaps a non-terminal session's checkpoint. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:80; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:337-338
USEFUL [Traps "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population"]: saved re-filing the Sweeper Get cost.

### [spec.6.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed no finding. BECAUSE: the reliability traces of the round-5 change (SPEC-5z item 13) and of the whole staging hold. Item 13's adoption trigger matches SPEC-2a's adoption set less the states whose inbox is already migrated or DLQ-held (`resume_pending`, `awaiting_client_action`, pre-running). A podless `suspended` session is never adopted, so it gets no spurious `inbox_cleared`. An adopted held-pod `suspended` session gets the event correctly, because its in-memory inbox died with the old holder. ALTERNATIVES: none filed.
FACT: A pod orphaned by a crash between the SPEC-5b step 2 binding clear and the pod release has a reclaimer. The §4.6.1 **Live binding states** predicate drains a claim whose pod no session's `pod_assignment` names once `claimOrphanTimeout` passes. Credential leases left unreturned there expire by `leaseTTLSeconds`. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517, :1253.
WATCHOUT: The accepted release-migration window ([spec.1.fix-design-G1.1] WATCHOUT, review-log.md:939) leaks more than a sender notification. §7.3 **DLQ drain on terminal transition:** deletes the DLQ key once (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:444). TTL expiry is enforced only in recovering states. So entries migrated after a serving-replica terminal drain (SPEC-2d item 2) stay in Redis with no reclaimer. I still did not file it, because the window is one Redis pipeline after a commit and no path widens it. Refile only with a widening path, or if a later round decides a permanent key leak is enough. The remedy is to extend SPEC-5i **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** to the release migration (re-read; if terminal, remove the entries and emit `target_terminated`).
USEFUL [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]: it recorded the clear-then-migrate ordering and why migrate-before-clear duplicates delivery, which settled the terminal-race question without re-deriving it.

### [spec.6.review-security.1]

FACT: Credential leases are per session on every pod, so a slot release at suspension (SPEC-5b step 2, concurrent pool) revokes only that session's leases and does not touch a sibling's. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:26 (**Per-session credential lease lifecycle.**).
FACT: The mid-resume snapshot-close step 3 sends the pod through the pool's default post-session scrub, and step 5 runs the session-wide credential release. A podless-`suspended` terminate served off-holder (SPEC-2d item 2) that lands on a `resuming` row therefore still revokes the attempt's leases, and the attempt releases its own pod when its next write misses. The failure mode is closed. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:220-222.
FACT: The security bounds this proposal touches are all held by trusted components. The suspended lifetime and the idle and backlog predicates read Postgres `SuspendedAt`, `SuspendedReason`, and `last_agent_activity_at`, which the gateway writes. Budget keys keep the §8.3 sentinel, so a lapsed key fails closed in the spec. Retry accounting advances on gateway-observed failure edges. None of these bounds comes from a pod self-report. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:406-408; spec-changes.md SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.**, SPEC-5g, SPEC-5o.
USEFUL [Standing context Open "Redis DLQ and inbox keys outside §12.8 user erasure"]: it stopped a refile of the erasure-scope gap, which RECORDS-1 7(k) records with the owner's default applied.

No staged text runs a periodic per-row probe over podless `suspended` rows. **Backlog resume.** evaluates held-pod rows on the coordinating replica only, plus one check at the release. ### [f1.human-decisions]
DECISION: OD-P1 resolved as the staged default: the Sweeper releases the mirror row of a podless `suspended` session on each sweep while this replica is the prior holder, until the lease lapses. No staged text was written (rung 1): CODE-5 item 1 builds it and the tier-1 test `TestSweepPodlessSuspendedHoldsNoLease_spec_10_1_1` pins it. The OD-P1 entry was removed from summary.md `## Open decisions for human to make`. BECAUSE `coordlease.Store.Upsert` clears `released_at` and `Release` acts only where `released_at IS NULL`, so a one-time release in the suspension release is undone by a sweep that listed the row before the pod was cleared, and nothing releases it again. ALTERNATIVES: the suspension release marks the mirror released once (rejected: leaves a stale §10.1.8 barrier-target row after the race, and adds a second writer to a register whose writer is the gateway sweeper). FACT: The DLQ Redis key carries no key-level EXPIRE in the tree. Only `RedisInbox.Expire` exists, and CODE-4 item 18 deletes it. A 7-day podless hold therefore does not lose DLQ entries to key expiry. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/sessioninbox.go:273, dlq.go:150
FACT: The per-message `last_agent_activity_at` write is not new write amplification. The shipped §6.2 already writes it to Postgres on every client message. SPEC-5f makes the write synchronous; it does not add a write. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:265-267
OPEN (non-spec loop, performance): CODE-6 item 14 says "A session that is not a tree root matches no key, so no root check is made" (non-spec-changes.md:213). As written, every entry to and exit from `suspended` for every session runs `RearmTree`, and each call is a keyless SCAN that iterates the whole keyspace of the Redis node owning the `{sessionID}` hash slot. With idle suspension on by default at 900 s, a root predicate (or a Postgres lineage check) would make this cost proportional to the number of tree roots instead of to the number of suspensions. The spec does not require SCAN, so the fix does not belong in this loop. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:474-485
WATCHOUT: The extra warm-pool claims and etcd writes from idle-suspend/resume cycles (spec/04 etcd estimate at :467-475 and the §17.8.2 claim rate cover session creation only) have no spec budget that the staging breaks. They were weighed and not filed, because the bar treats them as a design choice. Refile only with a stated spec ceiling that the staging exceeds. EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1040, :1089
USEFUL [Settled "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population"]: It scoped this pass and kept me from refiling the Sweeper `List` cost.

### [spec-recheck.1.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in spec-recheck round 1. BECAUSE the r4-prefix delta (SPEC-2d item 5 §15.1 events row, SPEC-5z item 13 Crash recovery row) adds no recovery mechanism. The traced paths all have a staged outcome: podless `suspended` → serving-replica `resume_pending` → lapsed-lease adoption (SPEC-2a) → driver; release crash between clear and pod release (WPC orphan-claim GC, lease TTL); a crashed attempt (watchdog plus GC); terminate or expiry racing an enqueue (SPEC-5i **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.**); and credential leases (returned at release, re-assigned after the entry write) — ALTERNATIVES: the SPEC-5f fail-open admission against the idle suspend write. Not refiled, per the review-log WATCHOUT at review-log.md:1081: a lock-wait timeout does not widen the window past one row-lock hold.
FACT: The DLQ code sets no key-level EXPIRE (pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go has only per-entry score expiry through sweepExpiredScript), so held messages of a 7-day podless `suspended` session are not silently aged out at the key level — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:137-147
FACT: §7.2 "Inbox drain on terminal transition" covers both the in-memory and the Redis-backed inbox, so the durable inbox of a podless `suspended` session that expires at its lifetime has a reclaimer even though the inbox key has no EXPIRE — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:352
UNVERIFIED: §10.1.4 `AdapterTerminating` handling writes `resuming → resume_pending/awaiting_client_action` and is not among SPEC-1b's listed `resuming` exits. I read it as the attempt's own failure report, because only the attempt holds the replacement pod's connection. A lifecycle-lens reviewer could confirm that reading. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:58

### [spec-recheck.1.review-security.1]
DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE the delta since spec-r7 is only SPEC-2d item 5 (§15.1 events row) and the SPEC-2 file list, neither of which touches a security control; the whole-staging walk (lease rule SPEC-2a, fence skip SPEC-2b, podless §29.3 rows SPEC-2d, credential leases SPEC-1g/SPEC-1f item 3, suspension release SPEC-5b, idle admission SPEC-5f, budget keys SPEC-5o) found no regression of a mandatory control and no security bound sourced from a pod self-report — ALTERNATIVES: filing the SPEC-2d item 3 podless interaction-resolution "records the resolution" text (rejected: a human-made resolution, no gate bypassed, and the §29.3 "never reports a successful outcome" sentence is scoped to forwarding rows); filing the SPEC-1f item 3 cross-replica lease revocation (rejected: explicitly accepted residual, fails toward revocation, and leaked leases are bounded by leaseTTLSeconds at spec/04_system-components.md:1253).
FACT: credential leases carry their own TTL (default 3600 s, `expiresAt = issuedAt + min(leaseTTLSeconds, providerMaxTTL)`), so a lease assigned by a crashed restore attempt that never releases it is bounded without a 0092 mechanism — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1253-1263
FACT: PreMessageDelivery is scoped to `lenny/send_message` and stated as firing "before the gateway delivers"; running it at acceptance before the DLQ hold satisfies that text, so held-message delivery without an interceptor is not a bypass — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1148
