### [non-spec-recheck.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 4 step 4 releases through `releaseReportedBinding(ctx, row.ID, bind, DispositionCompleted)` over the `*BindResult` the Design binding check read, skipping step 5 on a skip — BECAUSE the lock orders step 4 only against `releaseEarlierBinding`; pointer identity under the lock also orders it against `completeResume`'s publish, since a Put can only follow `releaseEarlierBinding`'s Remove of the snapshot under the same lock — ALTERNATIVES: SandboxName == PodAssignment (a same-pod slot or a re-claimed recycled pod carries the old name, so it releases the replacement); a new compare-and-release executor method (second copy of the predicate); holding the lock across steps 3 to 4 (lock held over unbounded Redis/Postgres calls, still needs identity for another replica's driver).
DECISION: `releaseReportedBinding` gains a `disposition` parameter and a `bool` result (true only when `releaseExecutor` ran and returned nil); the funnel passes `DispositionFailed` and ignores the result — BECAUSE step 4 must stop before step 5 on both a skip and an error, and the helper today returns nothing and logs its own error.
WATCHOUT: a step-4 release that runs after a concurrent resume published removes the NEW binding, because `PodExecutor.Release` keys on session ID; the row is left `running` naming a released pod and no stream-failure report follows (closedByGateway) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go `unbind`, `Release`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go `releaseReportedBinding`.
CORRECTS [review-log FACT "PodExecutor.Release on an unbound session returns nil ... revokes no new leases"]: true only when step 4 runs before the attempt's publish; after the publish an unconditional release finds the replacement binding and revokes its leases. The identity test in step 4 closes it.

### [non-spec-recheck.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 4 step 4 releases through `releaseReportedBinding` (given a disposition parameter) with the `*BindResult` the Design binding check read, under `slotAccountLocks` — BECAUSE the existing helper already tests "registry still returns this exact *BindResult" (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:276-284), and under the lock that test is atomic against a resume: completeResume can publish only after its releaseEarlierBinding (same lock, resume_rebind.go:168-178) has removed the snapshotted entry — ALTERNATIVES: comparing registry SandboxName with row.PodAssignment (the reviewer's suggestion) was rejected, see WATCHOUT; a new compare-and-release method on PodExecutor was rejected as a second copy of releaseReportedBinding's predicate.
WATCHOUT: a sandbox-name identity test does not discriminate. On a slot pool the resume can take a new slot on the same pod, and on a recycling exclusive pool releaseEarlierBinding returns the pod to idle and the same session's resume can re-claim it; both publish a binding whose SandboxName equals the old PodAssignment, and step 4 would release it. Pointer identity of the *BindResult is the test that discriminates — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindrequest.go:130-143 (SandboxName, SlotID); resume_rebind.go:124 (fresh result.Result Put).
CORRECTS [review-log FACT at ~line 1489, "PodExecutor.Release on an unbound session returns nil ... revokes no new leases"]: true only while the resume has not yet published. Once completeResume Puts the replacement binding the session is bound again and an unguarded step-4 `releaseExecutor` (keyed on session ID, executor/pod.go:202-208, :257-262) unbinds and releases the NEW binding and revokes its leases. The lock alone orders step 4 against releaseEarlierBinding, not against the later publish.
FACT: `releaseReportedBinding` has exactly one caller today (failure.go:239), so adding a disposition parameter touches one call site.
WATCHOUT: a skipped step 4 emits nothing in step 5; the pod was released by the resume's releaseEarlierBinding with DispositionFailed, not by the suspension release. SPEC-5b §6.2 step 2 prose and the SPEC-5q metric row stay true.

### [non-spec-recheck.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: Close the stream-failure-vs-zero-hold-release race with a `suspended`-source guard inside the funnel's edge mutators (`r.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment && s.holdsPod(*r)`, else `errReportConflict`), staged as a CODE-4 item 22 bullet plus tier-1 `TestFailureReportOnReleasedSuspendedRowIsNoOp` — BECAUSE `store.Get` can read a stale held-pod row, so only the primary-side compare in the mutator covers both a read after release step 3 and one before it; §6.2 already says pod failure while suspended-without-pod is impossible, so no spec text — ALTERNATIVES: early return in `ReportSessionFailure` (misses the stale read); guard on every source state (changes `resuming`, whose driver writes PodAssignment); Edge-cases entry accepting the race (contradicts §6.2); taking `slotAccountLocks` before the read (per-replica, report may be on another replica).

### [non-spec-recheck.3.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: The stream-failure race on a podless `suspended` row is real in the tree: `ReportAttachStreamFailure` checks the registry (`reportedStreamBinding`) before `store.Get`, `ReportSessionFailure` routes `suspended` to `applyFailureFromActive`, and the three edge mutators (`transitionToResumePending`, `transitionToAwaitingClientAction`, `transitionToFailed`) guard only `r.State != row.State` and stamp `rep.PodAssignment` back onto the row. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/stream_failure.go `ReportAttachStreamFailure`; failure.go `ReportSessionFailure` switch, `transitionTo*` mutators.
DECISION: Close it with one bullet in CODE-4 item 22: from a `suspended` source each edge mutator returns `errReportConflict` unless `r.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment && s.holdsPod(*r)` — BECAUSE the in-mutator check runs on the primary and covers both windows (a read after release step 3, and a held-pod read before step 3 or from the lagging read replica) with one predicate and the existing no-op disposition — ALTERNATIVES: a pre-mutator `!holdsPod(row)` return in `ReportSessionFailure` (rejected: `store.Get` reads the replica, so it misses the stale held-pod read; adding it beside the mutator guard states the rule twice); applying the PodAssignment equality to every source state (rejected: `resuming` shares these mutators and the driver may write PodAssignment within `resuming`, so the guard would need its own analysis); taking `slotAccountLocks` around the funnel's read (rejected: lock does not order the row read against step 3's commit on another replica).
WATCHOUT: Existing `TestReportSessionFailureReleasesTheBindingOnEveryEdge_spec_7_3` seeds a `suspended` row with empty PodAssignment, but `newReportFixture` wires no PodBinder, so `holdsPod` is true and the test stays green. A new test for the guard must wire a binder or it discriminates nothing. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure_test.go `newReportFixture`, loop at the `for _, from := range` over running/input_required/suspended.

So a binding the suspension release read can be replaced only by a resume on the same replica; the pointer-identity skip in CODE-6 item 4 step 4 cannot strand a binding through a non-resume republish. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:187-208,257-272
WATCHOUT: The failure funnel's edge mutators guard only on `r.State != row.State`, and ReportAttachStreamFailure checks the binding before it reads the row. Filed this round. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:329-337; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/stream_failure.go:53-73; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:236

### [non-spec-recheck.5.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE the round-4 delta (TestFailureReportOnReleasedSuspendedRowIsNoOp made table-driven over the three item 22 mutators) matches the code: transitionToFailed answers errReportConflict with a no-op disposition (failure.go:417-435), as the other two transitions do. A whole-proposal trace of REG-COORDLEASE across held-pod suspension, the zero-hold release, podless suspension, the hold-then-enterResumePending write, adoption of resume_pending, and the restore found no new loss, double-apply, leak without reclaimer, or stall. Credential leases are returned at the release and minted fresh at restore, and the release and the funnel both hold slotAccountLocks — ALTERNATIVES: refiling commit-repeat pacing (refuted earlier, review-log.md:431), the 7-day suspended listing (Traps, refuted), and the podless crash residual (OD-P3, an open decision).
FACT: PodExecutor.Release unbinds and evicts the stream without waiting for the reader goroutine, and endConn skips the stream-failure handler for an end the gateway caused. Holding slotAccountLocks across releaseExecutor in the funnel and in suspension-release step 4 therefore cannot deadlock against the funnel's own lock wait on the reader goroutine — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:257-270, podstream.go:331-353
FACT: Binder.Release and Binder.ReleaseSlot call releaseCredentials(sessionID) before any teardown that can fail, so a release error at suspension-release step 4 still returns the session's leases — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:60-66, slotbinder.go:783
FACT: credassign.Service holds leases in process, keyed by session. A crash between release steps 3 and 4 leaves leases that the next session-wide release returns; this is the same crash class as every other release path and is not new to 0092 — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:400-409
WATCHOUT: on a held-pod row expired by sweepSuspended on a non-holder, or on a running row expired by sweepMaxAge, the holder's binding is released only through the terminal hook on the expiring replica, which holds no binding. This predates 0092 (sweepMaxAge has the same shape). Do not file it against 0092 without a path the proposal adds — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:308-313

FACT: Lease renewals stay bounded by sessions that hold a pod plus `resume_pending`, `resuming`, and `awaiting_client_action` (900 s cap). EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:259-266; spec-changes.md:167
FACT: The shipped Sweeper lists every session row, including terminal rows, per tenant per replica on each sweep, and it does one `leases.Get` per non-terminal row. CODE-5 item 1 keeps that Get for podless `suspended` rows (OD-P1 default). This is a code-level cost under an open decision and has no spec site. FACT: The idle-suspension checkpoint and the resume claim scale at most with concurrent sessions divided by `maxClientIdleSeconds`, which is about 11/s each at Tier 3 against the 17/s periodic checkpoint budget and about 30 claims/s. The etcd and MinIO tier figures stay within budget. EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1222; spec/04_system-components.md:469-475

This becomes material only when a root spent more than one day (8 d TTL minus 7 d lifetime) outside `suspended` since that re-arm. It was judged below the bar and is owned by the security/performance lenses.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: readopted bindings carry SlotID/Recycle/CleanupCommands/CleanupTimeoutSeconds by resolving the pool inside `readoptAndFence` pinned to `row.PoolRef` (CODE-5 item 2, first bullet) — BECAUSE every release (`PodExecutor.Release`) dispatches on the binding alone, so a correct binding makes CODE-6 item 4 step 4 and SPEC-5b step 2 true and also fixes the pre-existing readopted running-row session-end drain — ALTERNATIVES: release-time pool lookup in `ReleaseSuspendedPod` (second source; leaves running-row readopt draining); RECORDS-1 filing (records a regression CODE-5/CODE-6 make reachable); tier-5 case (tier-1 `SlotID == sessionID` discriminates).
FACT: `readoptAndFence` builds `BindResult{SessionID, TenantID, SandboxName, PodIP, Adapter, CoordinationGeneration}` with no SlotID/Recycle; `PodExecutor.Release` sends empty-SlotID bindings to `Binder.Release` (whole-pod drain). Slot binds use `SlotID == SessionID`. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:253-260; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:263-271; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:150
WATCHOUT: the resume-rebuild path resolves on `row.Pool` (client pin, may be empty); the readopt resolve must pin `row.PoolRef`, the pool the pod is actually in. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:77-81
DECISION: new CODE-4 item 23 stops renewal of a released lease through a `credassign.Assigner.OnReleased` observer, `credRenewalWiring.forget`, and `credrenewal.ErrLeaseReleased` (replacing `errNoPoolBinding`) — BECAUSE nothing in production calls `Worker.Forget` and `Renew` re-mints any tracked lease, so Decision 8's returned capacity was false and idle suspension makes the release mainline — ALTERNATIVES: lease-store existence check in `Renew` (pgstore `GetByID` reads a DB error as not-found and would silently stop live renewals); session-liveness check (suspended/failed-attempt sessions are live); RECORDS-1 filing (regression the proposal makes mainline); homing in CODE-6 item 4 (same re-mint follows CODE-4 items 5.3, 20.4, and terminal releases).
FACT: `Worker.Tick` unlocks `w.mu` before calling `Renew`, and re-tracks `next` after it; a release landing between `Renew`'s recheck and the re-track is self-healing because the next `Renew` finds no pool binding and returns `ErrLeaseReleased` without minting. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:257-360
WATCHOUT: `Service.Release`/`ReleaseSession` hold `s.mu` across `releaseLocked`; the observer must fire after unlock (collect IDs), and `credRenewalWiring.Renew` must not call `Release` holding its own `w.mu`, since `forget` takes it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:380-414; cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:162-193
WATCHOUT: a release on a replica other than the one whose worker tracks the lease does not fire that worker's observer; the minting replica keeps re-minting until its own release. Same residual class as the handoff overlap (OD-C3); not staged. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/revocation.go:169-171 (per-replica OnAssigned)
OPEN: `credRenewalWiring.Renew` never releases the spent lease after a successful renewal, so each renewal leaves the old lease counted in `active` until session release. Pre-existing; changes live-session behavior; not part of this group.
FACT: `TestRenewWithoutPoolBindingFails` (cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal_test.go:186-197) doc comment says the worker "falls through to fault rotation"; item 23 makes that false, so the CODE-4 test bullet names its update. The design's cascade list missed it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: re-arm after every committed `enterResumePending` (both sources), in addition to the `suspended` entry and exit sites; SPEC-5o's clause reads "enters or leaves `suspended` or enters `resume_pending`"; the spec-changes Edge cases row accepting `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` for a client-cycled root is deleted — BECAUSE CODE-1 makes age accrue only in active states, the client `/resume` spends no retry, and treebudget's reserve script reads a missing key as 0 (silent budget reset); `RetryCount` is never reset, so pod-driven loops stay bounded by maxSessionRetries — ALTERNATIVES: building the §8.3 sentinel (RECORDS-1 7(d)) is a much larger mechanism; reserve-time TTL refresh misses a root that never delegates.
DECISION: `RearmTree(ctx, root, parentIDs)` makes no SCAN; it pipelines EXPIRE on named keys; the session server gates it on `ListByRoot(row.TenantID, row.ID)` and passes the distinct non-empty `ParentSessionID` of rows other than the row itself — BECAUSE SCAN MATCH walks the whole node keyspace, so a per-transition SCAN scaled with total keys times suspension rate past the Tier 3 ~400 ops/s delegation budget — ALTERNATIVES: an EXISTS `{root}:dlg:tokens` gate (that key is absent when TokenDelta is 0, see delegation/service.go Reservation); a `{root}:dlg:parents` set (new key and script change for state Postgres holds).

The staged text names the keys by suffix. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go `func (r Reservation) keys()`.

CORRECTS [review-log entry "It keys on the session's own id with `PurgeRoot`'s SCAN, so no root predicate is needed."]: `RearmTree` no longer SCANs; the session server lists the tree via `ListByRoot` and passes named parents. The standing context "`PurgeRoot` SCAN on a Redis Cluster client: CODE-6 item 14 `scanTree`" should read CODE-6 item 14's `PurgeRoot` routing bullet, and the test is now `TestPurgeRootRoutesToHashTagOwner`.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Readopted binding carries the release disposition: `readoptAndFence` resolves the pool pinned to `row.PoolRef` with `podsession.ResolvePool` (policy reader `sessionserver.NewPoolPolicyReader(w.pools)`), before the dial, and sets `SlotID = sessionID` when `MaxConcurrentSessions > 1`, plus `Recycle`, `CleanupCommands`, `CleanupTimeoutSeconds`, as `checkpointResumeRequest` copies them. One sentence in CODE-5 item 2, tier-1 test under CODE-5. BECAUSE the release reads only the binding (pod.go `Release` dispatches on `SlotID`; recycle reads `BindResult.Recycle`), so fixing the binding fixes every release of a readopted binding, including the pre-existing running/input_required one. ALTERNATIVES: resolve the pool inside `ReleaseSuspendedPod` (second source of the disposition; leaves the running-row readopt draining a shared pod at session end); file in RECORDS-1 7 (records a regression CODE-5/CODE-6 make reachable; Standing MISTAKE); a tier-5 concurrent-pool readopt-then-release case (tier 1 on the published `BindResult` discriminates; executor `SlotID` dispatch is existing tested code).
FACT: `SlotID` equals the session ID on every slot bind (slotbinder.go `ClaimSlot` doc, pod_claim.go comment), so the readopt seam can reconstruct it without a persisted slot column. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:150, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pod_claim.go:151
FACT: the readopt seam's binding has no `SlotID`/`Recycle` today, so a readopted running session on a concurrent pool already drains the shared pod at session end; unfiled in BUILD-GAPS. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:253-260, pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:263-271
WATCHOUT: `row.Pool` is the client pin (may be empty); `row.PoolRef` is the pool the session is bound to. Pin the readopt resolve on `PoolRef`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:75-79,459-462
DECISION: Released credential leases stop renewing through a release observer, new CODE-4 item 23. `credassign.Assigner` gains `OnReleased(func(leaseID string))` (mirrors `OnAssigned`, invoked outside the assigner's lock after the store removal, by `Service.releaseLocked` and `Client.Release`); the cmd wiring registers a `forget` that calls `Worker.Forget` and deletes the pool binding; `credRenewalWiring.Renew` treats a missing pool binding as released (`credrenewal.ErrLeaseReleased`, replacing `errNoPoolBinding`) before and after `Assign`, releasing the replacement after unlocking `w.mu` when the spent lease was forgotten mid-mint; `Worker.Tick` drops a lease on that sentinel with no retry, `OnExhausted`, or `OnRenewed`. BECAUSE Decision 8 / SPEC-1g say the release returns leases, and §4.9 Proactive Lease Renewal step 1 removes ended sessions' leases; today nothing calls `Forget` and `Renew` re-mints any tracked lease. ALTERNATIVES: a lease-store existence check in `Renew` (pgstore `GetByID` returns false on a DB error, so a transient read failure would silently stop renewing a live lease); a session-liveness check (a podless `suspended` session is live, so it would keep re-minting); RECORDS-1 filing plus qualifying Decision 8 (records a regression idle suspension makes mainline).
FACT: no production caller of `credrenewal.Worker.Forget`; `Renew` mints via `Assign` with no liveness check and never releases the spent lease. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:223-229, cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:162-200, cmd/lenny-gateway/revocation.go:169-171
FACT: the only `Assigner` satisfiers are `credassign.Service`, `credassign.Client`, and the fake in cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal_extend_wiring_test.go. EVIDENCE: grep `func (.*) OnAssigned(`
WATCHOUT: `Release` fires the observer, and the observer takes `credRenewalWiring.mu`; `Renew` must release the replacement after unlocking `w.mu` or it deadlocks.
WATCHOUT: residual not closed by the observer: a lease released by a replica other than the one that minted it (in-memory lease store per replica, or a release after a coordination-lease handoff while the minting replica lives) keeps renewing on the minting replica. Same handoff-overlap class as OD-C3; refile only with a routine path that releases on a non-minting live replica.
OPEN (separate finding, not staged): `credRenewalWiring.Renew` never releases the spent lease, so each renewal of a live session leaves one more lease counted in the credential's `active` until the session-wide release. Pre-existing; a later round may file it. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:177-193, pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:343

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: Close the client-cycled-root TTL gap by re-arming after every committed `enterResumePending` (both sources: `awaiting_client_action` and podless `suspended`), and delete the spec-changes Edge cases row that accepted the expiry — BECAUSE `RetryCount` is never reset on a successful restore (CODE-2 item 1 deletes the bump; no `RetryCount = 0` site in pkg/), so pod-driven `running`/`resume_pending` loops are bounded by `maxSessionRetries` and the only unbounded non-`suspended` loop is the client `/resume` edge, which always goes through `enterResumePending` — ALTERNATIVES: building the §8.3 sentinel check in CODE-6 (rejected: larger mechanism — sentinel, both scripts, cascade cancel, alert — and 7(d) stays a correct pre-existing record once the regression is unreachable at defaults); re-arming on every entry to any non-counting state (rejected: adds `awaiting_client_action` entries that change nothing about the bound).
DECISION: `RearmTree` drops the SCAN. The session server lists the tree with `Store.ListByRoot(row.TenantID, row.ID)`, takes the distinct non-empty `ParentSessionID` of the rows other than the root's own, makes no Redis call when there is none, and otherwise calls `RearmTree(ctx, root, parentIDs)`, which pipelines EXPIRE on the tree-wide, hwm, grant, and per-parent keys by name — BECAUSE per-parent keys (`parallel_children:{parent}`, `children_total:{parent}`) cannot be named without the parent set, and Postgres already records it (idx_sessions_root); a no-tree session then costs one indexed read and zero delegation-Redis ops — ALTERNATIVES: EXISTS gate plus SCAN (still a full-node SCAN per real root); EXISTS `{root}:dlg:tokens` then ListByRoot (tokens is not created when `TokenDelta` is 0, delegation/service.go Reservation; and it spends a Redis op per transition); a `{root}:dlg:parents` set maintained by the reserve script (new key and script change for state Postgres already holds).
FACT: The delegating parent of a delegated child is the child row's `ParentSessionID` (mcptools.go `ParentSessionID: parentOf[id]`); the field is overloaded by derive (sessionstore.go `ParentSessionID` doc), so a derived root names a non-parent — exclude the root's own row. `tree_size` is the one key every Reserve increments (`TreeSizeDelta: 1`, delegation/service.go). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:143-168; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go:1295-1307
DECISION: Keep the `PurgeRoot` Cluster SCAN routing fix, narrowed to `PurgeRoot` with no `scanTree` extraction, and its test renamed `TestPurgeRootRoutesToHashTagOwner` — BECAUSE the routing defect is real (archive FACT on go-redis keyless SCAN round-robin) and dropping the bullet would orphan it with no carrier — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the bullet as obsolete and filing it in RECORDS-1 (rejected: same text cost, loses a reviewed fix).
CORRECTS [Settled **`RearmTree` rules.** review-log.md:171]: "It keys on the session's own id with `PurgeRoot`'s SCAN, so no root predicate is needed" is withdrawn; RearmTree EXPIREs named keys from the tree's `ListByRoot` parent set, with no SCAN.
CORRECTS [Settled **Budget keys.** review-log.md:79]: re-arm triggers are now entry to and exit from `suspended` plus every entry to `resume_pending` via `enterResumePending`; cite SPEC-5o rather than restating.
CORRECTS [WATCHOUT review-log.md:1759]: once the spec-changes cycling-root Edge case row is deleted, the runbook's "misconfiguration or Redis-failure" reading of BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED holds again at defaults; only its `delegation.budgets.*` knob pointer stays wrong.
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: if the non-spec loop may not edit spec-changes.md, SPEC-5o's re-arm clause ("enters or leaves `suspended`") must gain "or enters `resume_pending`", and the Edge cases row "A root session that cycles between `awaiting_client_action` and `resume_pending` without entering `suspended`" must be deleted; both are false once CODE-6 item 14 re-arms on every `enterResumePending`.

### [non-spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]
USEFUL [Standing context, Settled "Kubernetes surface"]: confirmed again. The proposal adds no CRD field, status write, finalizer, webhook, or request-path reconcile. Its only apiserver writes are by-name `SandboxClaim` DELETEs (CODE-3 item 3, CODE-4 items 5.3 and 20.4, CODE-6 item 4), all through the existing `podclaim.DeleteClaim`.
FACT: Deleting a claim while the pod projects `claimed` (binding state `bound`) drains and then terminates the pod. Only a claim deleted from `reserved` returns the pod to `idle`. So the CODE-3 "release after the claim" drains the freshly claimed warm pod rather than returning it to inventory. That fails closed and is not a defect. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:414-415 (occupancy projection list).
FACT: The orphan-claim GC's "no active session references the pod" check is `GetActiveSlotsByPod`, which counts every non-terminal row whose `pod_assignment` names the pod. A held-pod `suspended` row protects its claim. A podless `suspended` row, and a replacement pod before the commit, do not. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:742-757; cmd/lenny-controller/main.go:168-185.
WATCHOUT: A by-name `DeleteClaim` (no UID or resourceVersion precondition) is ABA-safe only when the pod cannot be re-claimed under the same `claim-<pod>` name in between. Every DELETE this proposal adds runs on a claim its own attempt holds, or after a GC drain that retires the pod, so none of them can hit a successor's claim. A new late DELETE on a reserved or rebound pod would not be ABA-safe, and finalize.go records why. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go:507-513; spec/04_system-components.md:420.

### [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: The crash-takeover readopt seam builds its BindResult with SessionID, TenantID, SandboxName, PodIP, Adapter, and CoordinationGeneration only. It sets no SlotID and no Recycle. PodExecutor.Release dispatches on SlotID (empty means Binder.Release, which drains the whole pod), and the recycle disposition reads BindResult.Recycle. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:253-260; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:257-271; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindrequest.go:139-150
WATCHOUT: CODE-5 newly adopts held-pod `suspended` rows through that readopt, and the CODE-6 suspension release then releases the readopted binding. On a concurrent pool this drains the shared pod, and on a recycling pool it drains rather than recycles, which contradicts SPEC-5b step 2. Running-session readopt already has the same gap, but nothing records it. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:163-165, :189; spec-changes.md:446
FACT: Terminate, delete, and interaction-resolution handlers carry no lease gate, so a podless `suspended` session's §29.3 rows have a server on any replica with no code change. The events JSON form reads SubscribeForTenant like the streaming form. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go:15-110; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/events.go:123,454
FACT: The DLQ enqueue script sets no key EXPIRE. A podless `suspended` DLQ entry leaves only through delivery, sweep, overflow, removal, or the terminal drain. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:66-78
FACT: Assigned credential leases are tracked for renewal through credAssign.OnAssigned, so leases that Binder.Resume mints through the same assigner are renewed without extra wiring. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/revocation.go:166-171

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: Filed CODE-6 item 14's `RearmTree` as a per-suspension full-keyspace SCAN — BECAUSE the spec-recheck OPEN (review-log.md:1328) explicitly deferred it to the non-spec loop and the history lists it neither fixed nor refuted; SCAN MATCH visits every key on the node, so each call costs ceil(N/256) SCAN commands for every session's entry to and exit from `suspended`, root or not; at Tier 3 (10,000 sessions, 900 s idle default, about 22 calls/s) the ~400 ops/s delegation concern (spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1202) is exceeded once the node holds about 4,600 keys — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it OPEN again (rejected: no later loop owns it).
FACT: Budget keys are never deleted at tree settle in code. The reserve script arms the TTL only when TTL == -1 (treebudget.go:179-181), and the only deleter is `PurgeRoot`, whose only caller is GDPR erasure (cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:528). Key lifetime therefore equals the TTL, which CODE-6 item 14 raises from 24 h to 691200 s. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:38-43, :474-500
OPEN: the 8x TTL raise multiplies retained delegation-budget keys (and Redis memory on a noeviction instance) by about 8 because spec/08:1116's settle-time cleanup is unimplemented. No spec memory figure for the delegation concern was found, so this was not filed; a reviewer with a tree-creation-rate estimate should check it against the Tier 3 8 GB/node row (spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1189-1193).
FACT: The lease store is wrapped in `leasestore.NewFailover` (cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:210), so the Design lease gate keeps working through a Redis outage via the Postgres fallback. Do not refile "lease gate fails closed on Redis outage".
WATCHOUT: CODE-1 item 1's migration `ADD COLUMN ... CHECK (...)` makes Postgres scan the whole `sessions` table under ACCESS EXCLUSIVE (measured: 0 ms without the CHECK, 511 ms with it, on a 3M-row temp table in lenny-postgres pg16). It also omits `IF NOT EXISTS` (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:447), but only 34 of 103 ADD COLUMN migrations in the tree use it. Neither was filed, because no spec migration-downtime bound exists.
USEFUL [review-log.md:1328 OPEN on RearmTree]: located the defect directly.
USEFUL [Standing context "Refuted: the 7-day suspended population", "Refuted: store-outage degradation"]: settled the Sweeper Get, HeldLen, and Redis-outage DLQ candidates without re-deriving them.

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The §4.9 renewal worker tracks every lease credassign mints (OnAssigned → track, cmd/lenny-gateway/revocation.go:169-171) and nothing in production calls `Worker.Forget` (only credrenewal_test.go:209). `credassign.ReleaseSession` removes leases from the store without telling the worker, and `Renew` re-mints from the same pool for the session with no liveness check (cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:177; onRenewed keeps tracking when no binding exists, :226). Renew also never releases the spent lease, so each renewal adds one `active` count (credassign.go:343). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:223-229, :351.
WATCHOUT: Any claim that a release "returns leases to the pool" holds only until the releasing replica's next renewal tick past RenewBefore. The suspension release (CODE-6 item 4 step 4) and ReleaseUncommitted hit this path. Filed this round as a design-defect against Decision 8 / SPEC-1g. EVIDENCE: summary.md:23, :103.
FACT: replica IDs carry a random suffix (cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_identity.go:17-28), so a restarted gateway never matches its old `priorHolder`. A same-ID restart cannot pin a bound-less lease. EVIDENCE: coordination_identity.go:21-27.
DECISION: did not file the missed budget-key re-arm on a crash between commit and RearmTree — BECAUSE the 1-day margin of the 691200 s TTL over the 604800 s lifetime absorbs one missed entry re-arm (time outside `suspended` since the last re-arm is bounded by active age, default cap 7200 s) — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a lost post-commit write; rejected because no expiry follows from it.
DECISION: did not file the readopt-dial-failure loop on a held-pod `suspended` row whose pod and coordinator both died — BECAUSE it is the 0060 adoption behavior for `running` rows extended unchanged, and the 7-day lifetime bounds it — ALTERNATIVES: filing a missing pod-failure report from readopt; rejected as pre-existing.

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: the client-driven `awaiting_client_action` -> `/resume` -> `resume_pending` -> `awaiting_client_action` cycle lets a root stay outside `suspended` past the 8 d budget-key TTL, and in code an expired key reads as 0, so the tree budget silently resets. BECAUSE today `sweepMaxAge` expires every non-terminal row at wall-clock age from `CreatedAt` (2 h default), so the TTL is unreachable at defaults, and CODE-1 removes that cap for the recovery states while CODE-6 item 14 re-arms only on `suspended` entry and exit. ALTERNATIVES: treating it as covered by RECORDS-1 7(d) (rejected: same pattern as the earlier MISTAKE "recording a regression the proposal causes as a pre-existing defect"); treating it as covered by the spec Edge case at spec-changes.md:26 (rejected: that acceptance relies on a fail-closed sentinel that does not exist in code).
FACT: `sweepMaxAge` compares `now.Sub(row.CreatedAt)` for every non-terminal state today. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1074-1086
FACT: The delegation-budget Postgres checkpoint is restored only on a Redis-recovery edge (`Reconciler.Reconcile`) and never on a key's TTL expiry, so it is not a durable fallback for an expired key. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationbudget/reconciler.go:143-200
FACT: No production caller sets `PodSpiffeURI` on `BindRequest` or `SlotBindRequest`, so restore leases without it match create-time leases. EVIDENCE: grep of pkg/ and cmd/ for PodSpiffeURI
CORRECTS [non-spec.4.review-reliability FACT "time outside `suspended` is bounded by the 2 h age cap plus the 900 s windows"]: this holds only when the client does not resume. Each `/resume` from `awaiting_client_action` opens a new window and spends no retry, and CODE-1 accrues no age in the recovery states, so the client can extend the time indefinitely.
CORRECTS [non-spec-recheck-2.1.review-security.1 ALTERNATIVE "running-root budget keys outliving an 8-day TTL ... the 24 h arm-once TTL predates the proposal"]: the TTL predates the proposal, but the default-config reachability is new with CODE-1, and the BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED outcome is spec-only.
USEFUL [non-spec.5.review-security.1 WATCHOUT on spec-only fail-closed]: this pointed straight at the reserve script's read-as-0.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 item 23 stops renewal of a released lease by reading the shared lease store (`w.leases.GetByID`) for the spent lease before and after `Assign`, and both `ReleaseSession` implementations list the session's leases a second time — BECAUSE the release removes from the store every replica shares (`llmLeases`, Postgres-backed when configured; executor_credentials.go:72-81, :135, :146; revocation.go:86 passes `w.llmLeases` to the wiring), so the stop holds across replicas and in every write-then-read interleaving — ALTERNATIVES: `Assigner.OnReleased` observer plus `credRenewalWiring.forget` (replica-local, unordered against OnAssigned and Renew's re-check); Release-then-forget(replacement) in Renew; a spent-to-replacement successor map; firing OnReleased before store removal; looping ReleaseSession until empty (unbounded); a session-state check in Renew (misses by-ID releases, couples to session store).
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce an in-process release observer to stop renewal. A release on replica B never reaches replica A's worker, and the observer's firing order against OnAssigned tracking and Renew's post-Assign check cannot be fixed. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:96-99 (per-replica `pools` map); credassign/credassign.go:268-278 (observer outside s.mu); credassign/client.go:337-347 (ReleaseSession snapshot then per-lease RPC before Remove).
WATCHOUT: A nil `w.leases` must count as "no record" (fail closed). Existing tests that build the wiring with a nil store (cred_renewal_test.go:105, :163, :192; cred_renewal_extend_wiring_test.go:365, :435) change to pass a store holding the renewed lease; do not relax the check to keep them green. — EVIDENCE: the staged test bullet for TestReleasedLeaseIsNotRenewed_spec_4_9.
FACT: `credleasestore.LeaseStore` exposes `GetByID(leaseID) (credential.Lease, bool)` and `LeasesBySession`; both assigners write to the same store the renewal wiring holds as `w.leases`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/credleasestore.go:32-38; cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:89.
MISTAKE: Round 1 of this loop staged item 23 as an `OnReleased` observer; G1 of round 2 found three separate holes in it (a release between mint and track, a ReleaseSession snapshot taken before the replacement was recorded, and a release on another replica) plus an untested Client path. The observer, `credRenewalWiring.forget`, and the Forget doc-comment rewrite are removed.
CORRECTS [review-log entry crediting CODE-4 item 23 with a `credassign.Assigner.OnReleased` observer]: item 23 now uses the shared-store read and the ReleaseSession second list; no observer exists.
OPEN: A by-ID `Release` of a lease that a renewal has already replaced (the caller holds the spent ID) leaves the replacement alive until session end; the design ruled it a separate finding. A later round should decide whether `ReleaseUncommitted`/attempt-scoped releases can hit this.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 item 23's renewal stop moves from a replica-local release observer to the shared lease store. `Renew` refuses (ErrLeaseReleased) when `w.leases.GetByID(spent)` has no record or the spent lease has no pool binding, re-reads the spent lease after `Assign` and releases the replacement when it is gone, and both `ReleaseSession` implementations list the session's leases a second time after their removals. `Assigner.OnReleased`, `credRenewalWiring.forget`, and the `Forget` doc-comment rewrite are deleted — BECAUSE the observer fires only on the releasing replica (finding 2) and its timing against `OnAssigned`/the post-check is unordered (findings 0, 1); the store is what every replica's release writes. The two writes form a Dekker pair (Renew: Put replacement, then read spent; release: remove spent, then re-list), so every interleaving is caught by one side. One re-list suffices: a replacement Put after the spent removal is caught by Renew's post-check. — ALTERNATIVES: forget(replacement) inside Renew (finding 0's fix; still replica-local, misses finding 1 and 2); successor map spent→replacement in w.pools (replica-local, new state); firing OnReleased before store removal (still replica-local); a loop-until-empty re-list (unbounded; one re-list is provably enough).
FACT: The renewal wiring's `w.leases` is the same `llmLeases` store both assigners Put into and Remove from (Service via `credassign.New(llmLeases, ...)`, Client via `ClientOptions.Leases`), Postgres-backed whenever pgPool is set. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:71-86,133-146; cmd/lenny-gateway/revocation.go:86
FACT: Both `OnAssigned` observers (Service and Client) run synchronously before `Assign` returns, so by the time `Renew`'s post-check runs the replacement is already tracked; a released-but-tracked replacement is dropped by the store pre-check at its own RenewBefore, so no `Forget` call is needed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:268-278; client.go:164-171
WATCHOUT: Five renewal tests in cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal_test.go (lines ~105, 163, 222, 279, 322) and two in cred_renewal_extend_wiring_test.go (~365, 435) build the wiring with a nil lease store and expect a mint. Under the store check (nil counts as no record, fail closed) they must pass a store holding the renewed lease. Do not make a nil store skip the check: that is the leak.
WATCHOUT: In the no-Postgres mode the lease store is per-replica in-memory, so the cross-replica stop does not hold there; that mode does not resolve leases across replicas at all. — EVIDENCE: executor_credentials.go:67-71
OPEN (separate finding, not staged): a by-ID release (`releaseAttemptCredentials` in ReleaseUncommitted/failResume, Token Service `Release`) of a lease whose renewal already passed its post-check leaves the replacement live and renewing; neither the old observer design nor the store design catches it, because the release names only the spent ID. Requires leaseTTL - renewBeforeBuffer shorter than the attempt; reachable with a small `leaseTTLSeconds`.
CORRECTS [review-log 1834, 1836, 1837, 1866, 1869, 1899]: the `OnReleased` observer, its after-unlock WATCHOUTs, and "cross-replica is the same residual class" are superseded; the stop now reads the shared lease store and the cross-replica case is closed, not residual.
CORRECTS [review-log 1835]: "self-healing because the next Renew finds no pool binding" — the binding is replica-local and is moved to the replacement by a successful Renew; self-healing now comes from the store pre-check.

### [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 item 23 keeps the shared-store release test and makes absence authoritative: a new `LeaseStore.Lookup(leaseID) (Lease, bool, error)` (pgstore: not-found only on `pgx.ErrNoRows`; scan, KMS, and decode failures are errors that `Renew` returns as ordinary retryable failures), and new sub-item 4: `onExtend` advances the row's `ExpiresAt`/`RenewBefore` on both delivery modes through a new update-only `LeaseStore.Advance(lease) (bool, error)`, direct branch before the RPC; `Advance` finding no row returns `ErrLeaseReleased`, which `Tick` drops as sub-item 3 states. A post-`Assign` lookup error releases the replacement, deletes only its pool binding, and returns the error. BECAUSE the direct branch never wrote the row, so the sweep removed an extended live lease's row and `Renew` read that as a release; and `scanLease` folds every failure into ok=false. ALTERNATIVES: skip the pre-Assign read for extended leases (the post-Assign read still misreads the swept row once the breaker closes); advance via `Put` (an upsert that resurrects a lease a concurrent release removed between `GetByID` and `Put`, which defeats item 23 and, for proxy rows, re-validates a released token); change `GetByID`'s signature (dozens of callers).
FACT: Shipped `onExtend` already fails a direct-mode lease's second extension once the sweep removed its row (`GetByID` miss -> error -> recordFailure -> exhaust into the Fallback Flow), so the §4.9 cap teardown was effectively unreachable in direct mode; item 23 sub-item 4 fixes that as a side effect. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go `onExtend` (GetByID before the delivery-mode switch); credential_lease_sweep.go `sweepExpiredCredentialLeases`; pgstore.go DeleteExpired `expires_at < $1`.
WATCHOUT: The shipped proxy branch of `onExtend` is a `GetByID` then `Put` upsert and can resurrect a row a concurrent release removed; sub-item 4's `Advance` replaces it. Do not "simplify" `Advance` back to `Put`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go `Put` (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE).
WATCHOUT: With the row tracking the worker's `ExpiresAt`, a sweep can remove a live row only under cross-replica clock skew larger than the renew-before buffer, because `Tick` exhausts any lease past its tracked `ExpiresAt` before calling `Renew`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go `Tick` expiresAt guard.
CORRECTS [review-log DECISION lines 1834 and 1866, ALTERNATIVES "lease-store existence check"]: the objection (pgstore `GetByID` reads a DB error as not-found) is now answered by `Lookup`'s error return, not by avoiding the store read.
FACT: Citer sweep over "item 23", `ErrLeaseReleased`, `GetByID`, `Lookup`, `Advance`, and "renewal stop" across all proposal files: the checklist S12 and summary CODE-4 rows only name "the renewal stop for a released lease" and stay true; no other site describes sub-items 2-4. spec-changes.md untouched.

### [non-spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: credassign observer ordering. `Service.Assign` stores the lease under `s.mu` in `assignLocked` and invokes the OnAssigned observer only after unlocking; `Client.Assign` likewise records the lease (`recordAssignedLease`) inside `c.assign` and calls the observer afterwards. A release that runs in that window removes the lease before `track` registers it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/credassign.go:268-279, client.go:160-168
WATCHOUT: CODE-4 item 23 fires OnReleased only "for each lease they remove". A lease released between its mint and its OnAssigned `track` therefore gets OnReleased before it is tracked, and nothing forgets it afterwards. Renew's re-check then calls `Release(replacement)`, which is a no-op and fires nothing, so the replacement stays tracked and is re-minted at its RenewBefore. Any fix has to make Renew forget the replacement itself, or make `track` refuse a lease the store no longer holds. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:156-158; cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:128-138
FACT: `Worker.Tick` does not hold `w.mu` while it calls `Renew`, so the observer's `Worker.Forget` and `Track` called from inside Renew do not deadlock. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:257-303
FACT: the treebudget key set is the five reserve keys plus `parallel_children_hwm` and `token_grant`, and no other package writes `{root}:dlg:*`. CODE-6 item 14's no-SCAN `RearmTree` therefore covers every key, given the parent ids. — EVIDENCE: treebudget.go:218-254, checkpoint.go:28-33
CORRECTS [Standing context Traps "`RearmTree` rules."]: RearmTree no longer keys on the session's own id with PurgeRoot's SCAN. Since non-spec r1 it lists the tree with `ListByRoot(root)`, takes the distinct `ParentSessionID` values, and pipelines EXPIRE with no SCAN (non-spec-changes.md:221-222). The "no root predicate" clause is stale.
FACT: readopt pool resolution. `ResolvePool` needs the k8s reader, the namespace, `RuntimeRef`, the isolation profile, and a pin. `PoolRef` is written only at create, delegated materialize, derive, and the experiment router, never by a resume commit. Pinning to `PoolRef` matches the pool the unpinned resume resolution picks unless two pools serve the runtime, in which case the resume itself fails with ErrAmbiguousPool. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:282; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:80-81; experimentrouter.go:248-253

### [non-spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: `Client.Release` runs the RevokeCredentials RPC before `c.leases.Remove`, and `Client.ReleaseSession` lists `LeasesBySession` once up front. `Service.ReleaseSession` holds `s.mu` across list and removal. CODE-4 item 23 fires the observer after the removal and outside the lock, so the observer for a spent lease can fire after `credRenewalWiring.Renew` has re-checked its binding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:299-345; credassign.go:380-409.
WATCHOUT: Renew's post-Assign binding re-check (CODE-4 item 23.3) does not close the race in which the release lists the session's leases before `Assign` records the replacement and its observer for the spent lease fires after the re-check. The replacement then stays tracked and bound, and it is re-minted at every RenewBefore. A successor record (spent to replacement) that `forget` releases closes it. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:163-195; pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:303-357 (Renew runs outside w.mu, and re-tracking happens after Renew returns).
FACT: The ListByRoot re-arm (CODE-6 item 14) costs one indexed read-replica query per suspend or resume transition (idx_sessions_root, maxTreeSize 50). This is not a top-tier bottleneck. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:647-676; migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:101; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:125.
FACT: The readopt `ResolvePool` adds one poolstore Get and a CRD-cache list per takeover, and it runs only after the `RecordHandoff` Postgres CAS, so a Postgres failover fails the takeover before the resolve. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pool_policy_mirror.go:34-60; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resolve.go:282.

### [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed one finding on CODE-4 item 23 (renewal stop for a released lease): the Renew post-check keys on the spent lease's pool binding, which the release removes only in the OnReleased observer that runs after the release's lease snapshot (and, on credassign.Client, after each RevokeCredentials RPC), so a replacement minted between the snapshot and forget(old) is neither released nor refused — BECAUSE Client.ReleaseSession snapshots LeasesBySession then RPCs per lease (client.go:337-347, :299-326), and the production gateway uses Client whenever --token-service-addr is set (cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:127-139) — ALTERNATIVES: cross-replica renewal (minting replica != releasing replica) not filed: needs a live stale replica, and the gap predates 0092.
FACT: credrenewalWiring.Renew mints the replacement but never releases the spent lease; it only moves the pool binding (cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:160-200). The spent lease stays in the store until a ReleaseSession. Pre-existing; not 0092's to fix — EVIDENCE: cred_renewal.go:186-189.
FACT: Worker.Tick re-inserts tracked[next] after Renew returns even if Forget(next) ran meanwhile; harmless after item 23.3 because the re-tracked lease has no pool binding and its next Renew returns ErrLeaseReleased — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:351-354.
FACT: PodExecutor.Release unbinds the registry entry before calling the binder, so a release error in ReleaseSuspendedPod step 4 leaves no registry binding behind — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:257-271.
WATCHOUT: the item 22 funnel guard sends a stream-failure report on a podless suspended row to the no-op disposition, and the no-op path releases no binding ("A conflict or no-op runs neither step", failure.go:189-190); any future path that leaves a binding on a podless row has no other releaser — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:232-240.

### [non-spec.2.review-security.1]
FACT: In production the credential-lease store is the shared Postgres `credleasepg` store (cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:71-86), passed to both `credassign.Client` (as `Leases`) and the renewal wiring (`w.llmLeases`, revocation.go:86). The renewal `Worker`, its `pools` binding map, and every `OnAssigned` observer are per replica. A release on replica B removes the lease from the shared store, but only B's observers fire. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:96-99, pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:299-326
WATCHOUT: Under CODE-4 item 23 the renewal stop is keyed on the per-replica pool binding, so it holds only when the replica that tracks the lease also released it. After a dead-connection handoff (the Sweeper's evictBinding on A, readopt on B), B's suspension or terminal release leaves A's worker re-minting the lease. Filed as a finding with a shared-store check in `Renew` as the remedy. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:248-253
FACT: Nothing in the tree calls `credrenewal.Worker.Forget` today (grep of non-test Go). Item 23's premise is correct. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:225
DECISION: Did not file a rearm-failure budget reset. BECAUSE with default timers the gap between the last arm and a failed suspension-entry rearm stays well under the 1-day margin between the 691200 s TTL and the 604800 s lifetime. ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a lens-(2) store-outage reset was rejected as speculative.

### [non-spec.3.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings on the round-3 rewrite of CODE-4 item 23 — BECAUSE the new shared-store reads add two indexed `credential_leases` lookups per renewal and one extra `LeasesBySession` per session release. At the renewal rate (per lease per TTL minus buffer) and the release rate (session end or idle suspension), this is negligible beside the per-LLM-request `GetByToken` decrypt the proxy already pays. The Postgres or KMS failure mode of `Lookup` (an ordinary error, then 3 retries at 30 s, then OnExhausted) is no worse than shipped, because shipped `Renew` already writes the gateway store through `leases.Put` in both `Service.assignLocked` and `Client.recordAssignedLease` and takes the same retry path when that write fails — ALTERNATIVES: filing the Lookup-failure exhaustion as a failover regression (rejected: the shipped Put has the same Postgres and KMS dependency); filing the direct-mode `Advance` before a failed `ExtendCredentialLease` as pool overcount (rejected: bounded by one renewBeforeBuffer and no worse than the shipped undercount).

### [non-spec.3.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability finding filed on the round-2 rewrite of CODE-4 item 23 — BECAUSE the double list in ReleaseSession plus Renew's post-Assign Lookup close the release/renew race on a linearizable store (Put(replacement) precedes the post-Assign Lookup in program order, and Remove(spent) precedes the second list, so one of the two reads sees the other), and Lookup now separates ErrNoRows from scan/KMS/decode errors — ALTERNATIVES: filing the Lookup-failure branch's "release the replacement" (Release re-reads with GetByID, which pgstore turns into a silent no-op on the same store error) was rejected as below the bar: the leak is bounded by lease TTL in Client mode, and in Service mode it is the same class as the pre-existing active-counter leak of every renewed spent lease.
FACT: in Service mode a successful renewal never releases the spent lease; ps.active for it is decremented only if a later ReleaseSession lists the row before the expired-lease sweep deletes it (credassign.go:343, :414-423; credential_lease_sweep.go:82). Pre-existing; do not attribute it to 0092. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:162-206
FACT: the Sweeper already issues one leases.Get per non-terminal row per sweep on every replica (coordination.go:322), so CODE-5's podless skip placed after Get keeps a per-row Redis read over the podless suspended population. It is pre-existing and a capacity question for the performance lens, not a held-message probe; summary Decision 5's "no replica probes its held messages" stays true. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:303-327
WATCHOUT: §11.4 user revocation removes lease rows (user_revocation.go:185-188); after item 23 a peer whose Renew runs before the deny-list propagation reaches it drops the lease as ErrLeaseReleased rather than exhausting it. That is acceptable for a revoked user, but a future reviewer should not read "a missing record means a release" as covering only session releases. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:184-190
UNVERIFIED: Files touched on application (non-spec-changes.md, the list after Edge cases) omits pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/credleasestore.go, pgstore/pgstore.go, and tests/tier2_component/stores/credleasestore_test.go, which item 23 and its tier-2 test now edit. CODE-4 Targets names the first two. This is bookkeeping; a bookkeeping lens should confirm it.

DECISION: Off-holder lease-gate tests now require a pod binder wired; the release and backlog off-holder cases also require the session bound in the pod registry — BECAUSE the widened Design lease gate opens without a binder, and the Design binding check refuses before the lease read without a binding — ALTERNATIVES: binder qualifier alone, which leaves those two cases passing without exercising the gate.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the Design lease gate admits every session when no lease store OR no pod binder is wired — BECAUSE without a binder no session ever acquires REG-COORDLEASE, so Compose Mode (lease store wired, no binder) refused every held-pod resume — ALTERNATIVES: per-site binderless exceptions (five copies of one predicate), CODE-5 adoption of binderless podless rows (reintroduces podless adoption that redesign 5 removed), lease acquire at create without a binder (new lease traffic for a single-replica topology).
FACT: Compose Mode wires the lease store and no binder: docker-compose.yml:119-131 (--dev-mode, echo runtime, LENNY_REDIS_URL, no --agent-namespace); the binder is built only inside `if *agentNamespace != ""` — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:146,280; redis_quota.go:221; create_and_start.go:482 acquires the lease only when `bound != nil`.
WATCHOUT: the discriminating test is the CODE-4 item 8 tier-1 bullet's "lease store wired, no binder, no lease held" sentence; the tier-3 harness wires no lease store and does not discriminate the gate.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: Compose Mode wires the Redis lease store (and Sweeper) but no pod binder, so no session ever acquires REG-COORDLEASE there: the binder exists only under --agent-namespace with a cluster client, and the at-bind acquire runs only `if bound != nil`. EVIDENCE: docker-compose.yml:110-131 (LENNY_REDIS_URL, --dev-mode, echo); cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:146,280; cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:145,221; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go:482-497
DECISION: key the Design lease gate's admit-all case on "no lease store or no pod binder", the same binderless condition `holdsPod` uses, and pin it with one sentence on the existing CODE-4 item 8 tier-1 `handleResume` bullet — BECAUSE one predicate in the gate's one home reopens every lease-gated site (held-pod resume, REST/MCP held branch, ResumeBacklog, binderless DriveResume) at once — ALTERNATIVES: per-site "or no binder" exceptions (hair, five copies); having CODE-5 adopt binderless podless `suspended` rows (reintroduces adoption of podless rows, which redesign 5 removed); acquiring a lease at create without a binder (new mechanism for a single-process topology).
CORRECTS [non-spec-recheck.2.review-reliability.1]: its FACT that the lease gate "already refuses held-pod resume of binderless sessions under a wired lease store" described a defect, not a safe state; after this fix the gate admits every session when no pod binder is wired. The two UNVERIFIED entries on the lease-store-without-binder topology (review-log ~1790, ~1994) are answered: the topology is Compose Mode, and it was stranded.

### [non-spec.8.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the re-route after `sessionidle.ErrSessionSuspended` (CODE-6 item 7) re-reads the row on the primary as `holdMessage` does (CODE-4 item 7), and the first message-path re-read stays a `store.Get` — BECAUSE the first stale replica read is caught by the `StampSync` primary admission, while a re-route that re-reads the replica reclassifies `running` and loops until the replica catches up — ALTERNATIVES: every re-read on the primary (wider than the defect); a retry bound or backoff (a second mechanism that still classifies a stale row).
WATCHOUT: the re-route's discriminating test needs a store whose `Get` returns `running` after the row is `suspended`; memstore has no lag, so a plain memstore run passes on the old text. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Tests entry `TestHeldMessagesDeliveredBeforeNewMessage`.
FACT: the MCP path's `deps.Store` is the full `sessionstore.Store`, so it can run the `Update` primary re-read; `errHoldReread` is a `sessionserver` package sentinel, so the MCP package needs its own no-write sentinel or reaches the read through `SessionDelivery`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools.go:351

### [non-spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The re-route after `ErrSessionSuspended` (CODE-6 item 7) re-reads the row on the primary through the `errHoldReread` Update of CODE-4 item 7, cited rather than restated; the first re-read of each message path stays `store.Get` — BECAUSE a stale first read is caught by the `StampSync` admission (a primary Update), so only the re-route after a refusal can loop on replica lag — ALTERNATIVES: making every message-path re-read a primary read (wider, changes the existing `messages_delivery.go` re-read with no defect behind it); a retry bound or backoff on the re-route (hair: a second mechanism where the primary read removes the loop); renaming `errHoldReread` to a generic name (rename cascade for no implementor gain).
FACT: `pgstore.Get` reads `s.read`, which is the replica pool when `--pg-read-dsn` is set; `Update` runs on the primary — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:61, :356-357; the existing message-path re-read is `s.store.Get` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:249.
WATCHOUT: a memstore test cannot observe this; the discriminating case needs a store whose `Get` returns a stale `running` row while `Update` sees `suspended` (TEST list, `TestHeldMessagesDeliveredBeforeNewMessage`).

### [non-spec.8.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: the ErrSessionSuspended re-route in CODE-6 items 5.4 and 7 reclassifies a row the message path reads through store.Get, which is the read replica, so when the replica lags behind SuspendIdle's commit the path keeps classifying `running`, and StampSync keeps refusing on the primary. The loop has no bound, no backoff, and no primary read. BECAUSE: the same replica-staleness defect was already fixed for holdMessage's re-read (errHoldReread), and StampSync has just read the row on the primary as `suspended`. ALTERNATIVES: filing it as performance-only (rejected because the loop has no termination condition other than replica catch-up).
FACT: pgstore.Get, List, and ListByRoot read `s.read`, which is the --pg-read-dsn pool when that flag is set and the primary otherwise. Every "re-read the row" in the message paths, ResumeSuspended, DeliverHeld, and handleResume is a replica read unless the text says primary. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:61,356-357; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:465-471
FACT: §29.3 rows for a podless suspended session all have a server once SPEC-2d, SPEC-3j, and SPEC-5l land. Messages and resume use a store write on any replica. Terminate and delete go through handleTransition (store write) and recordSessionCompleted, which drains the DLQ; Checkpointer.Seal returns nil when the replica holds no binding. Resolution goes through resolveInteraction (interaction store). Events JSON is served from SubscribeForTenant's backlog, which merges the relay. Interrupt and upload do not admit `suspended`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go:59-111; pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:396-402; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interactions.go; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/events.go:440-460; spec/15_external-api-surface.md:645,648
FACT: the only REG-COORDLEASE acquirers are Sweeper.Sweep and Server.acquireCoordinationLease (callers: create_and_start, resume_rebind via resumeOnPod, which CODE-4 item 4 removes, delegated_child_materialize, and bind_registration/registerBinding). No message, resolution, or terminate path acquires a lease for a podless `suspended` session. EVIDENCE: grep `acquireCoordinationLease(` and `.Acquire(ctx` over pkg/gateway and cmd/lenny-gateway
USEFUL [standing-context Lease retention / 0091 lease behavior]: the renew path upserts the mirror on every sweep (coordination.go:491), so a podless row's released mirror is restored once a write moves it to resume_pending and the prior holder renews.

### [non-spec.8.review-performance.1]
FACT: Shipped `credRenewalWiring.Renew` (cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:162-205) reads only the in-memory `w.pools` map; item 23.2 adds two pgstore `Lookup`s per renewal (each a KMS-envelope `openLease`, pgstore.go:105). At tier 3 (~10,000 sessions, spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1280) renewals are a few per second, so the added Postgres/KMS reads are not a budget concern. `Assign` already writes Postgres, so the failover dependency is not new. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:181, pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:419
FACT: `w.pools` entries are deleted only by `Renew` success, `onExhausted`, and `onExtensionCapReached` (cred_renewal.go:191, :277, :346). After item 23, the first-read `ErrLeaseReleased` branch leaves the spent lease's entry in place. Shipped code already keeps one entry per released-lease chain forever, because it re-mints, so the entry count is no worse than shipped. Do not file this as a regression. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:135
WATCHOUT: `SuspendIdle` sits behind the lease gate without the Design binding check, so every replica reads REG-COORDLEASE once per tick (5 s, watchdog.go:166) for each over-idle `running` row whose turn is still in flight, for example a long silent tool call in direct mode. The cost is R×K/5 Redis GETs per second. That is far below any Redis budget, so it falls below the bar. Refile only with a spec-stated Redis ops budget that it exceeds. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 5.3; pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166
WATCHOUT: holdMessage's primary-side re-read (CODE-4 item 7) makes every held message answer `inbox_unavailable` during the ≤30 s Postgres primary failover (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:151-153). Shipped DLQ buffering needed no primary write. Messages are rejected rather than lost, so this is availability-only, which the standing "store-outage degradation" refutation covers. Refile only with a loss case.
USEFUL [Refuted: the 7-day suspended population]: it saved re-deriving the per-replica `sweepSuspended` listing cost (1.8M rows at 3 sessions/s × 7 days).

### [non-spec.8.review-single-source.1]
DECISION: no findings under the single-source lens in non-spec round 8 — BECAUSE the r2-prefix-to-now delta (item 23 renewal stop, the widened Design lease gate "no lease store or no pod binder", the orphan-reconciler guard, and the "on a server with a pod binder wired" test qualifiers) adds no second full statement of any rule. The gate predicate is written only in the Design **Session server** bullet, and summary Decision 1 ("or on every replica in dev mode") is a clause. holdsPod is defined only in CODE-4 item 8; CODE-6 item 2.2 restates it in one clause with a citation. — ALTERNATIVES: refiling the holdMessage result mapping (CODE-4 item 7 and the CODE-6 item 7 REST and MCP bullets), the Decision 11 and CODE-6 item 12 held-message outcome, and the no-periodic-evaluation reason (Design, CODE-6 item 2.2, Decision 5). All three were rejected because review-log entries :446, :1823, and :1988 already judged each one a citation plus summary.
FACT: The REG-COORDLEASE holding rule still has one home, SPEC-2a (spec-changes.md:166). Decision 5, the spec Design paragraph, the non-spec Design Sweeper bullet, and CODE-5 item 1 each cite or implement it. CODE-5 podlessSuspended (state suspended and an empty PodAssignment) matches SPEC-2a's "stops renewing ... when a suspended session's row stops naming a pod". — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:166; non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 item 1; summary.md:20
FACT: Credential-lease trace across suspend and resume. The suspension release goes through CODE-6 item 4 step 4, then releaseReportedBinding, then the session-wide releaseCredentials under slotAccountLocks. Item 23 stops re-mint of the released leases. The checkpoint restore assigns new leases (CODE-4 item 20, home SPEC-1g), and the rebuild assigns them through Bind/Prepare. Each rule has one home, and the lock argument in CODE-6 item 4 step 4 and in CODE-4 item 22 is per call site, with each citing the other. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 items 20, 22, 23; CODE-6 item 4
FACT: No docs page under docs/ describes which session states hold the coordination lease, so SPEC-2a has no docs mirror to restate it. — EVIDENCE: grep of docs/ for "coordination lease|REG-COORDLEASE" returns only docs/reference/glossary.md:359
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-single-source.1]: its lease-rule and backlog-reason inventory still held, so only the later delta needed re-derivation.

### [non-spec.8.review-test-coverage.1]
DECISION: no test-coverage finding filed for round 8 — BECAUSE every staged behavior (CODE-1 to CODE-6 items, item 23 renewal stop, CODE-5 podless-suspended skip and mirror release, SPEC-2d podless terminate/events rows, path-6 hold outcomes, backlog check at release) maps to a named test at the tier it reaches, with the deny, guard-miss, store-error, and race cases listed — ALTERNATIVES: filing the unqualified holdsPod-false tests (TestPodlessMessageEnqueuesBeforeResumePending, TestPath6PodReleasedBeforeResumeTakesPodReleasedBranch, the line-412 podless-branch case, the !holdsPod handleResume case, TestReleaseSuspendedPodChecksBacklogAfterClear's resume_pending case) as missing "pod binder wired" qualifiers; rejected because each of them FAILS rather than passes vacuously without a binder (holdsPod is never false binderless), so the expected outcome itself forces the fixture. Only a no-op assertion that would pass for the wrong reason without a binder needs the qualifier, and the one such test (TestFailureReportOnReleasedSuspendedRowIsNoOp) already carries it.
FACT: every existing test the Testing section names for rewrite or deletion exists in the tree under that exact name (checked 36 names, incl. TestCrossReplicaResumeAfterStreamFailureHoldsTheRow_spec_7_3 at tests/tier4_integration/stream_failure_report_test.go:545 and TestIdleSweepDefaultIsAgeCap_spec_6_2 in pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/idle_test.go). — EVIDENCE: grep of func names over *_test.go
FACT: TEST-0's preamble requirement survives SPEC-3i and SPEC-5l, which edit other sentences of the same §29.3 lead; the interaction-resolution row's required phrases survive the SPEC-2d item 3 append. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/off_holder_matrix_stated_outcome_test.go:67-75,125-133
